DoMS Seminars

Wednesday, October 14th, 2026 at 12:00 (IST)

Speaker: Dr. Shatabdi Sonkar

Details of Seminar: Consumer Behaviour Towards the Purchase of Green Cosmetic Products in India
Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/95913520257?pwd=NHiOYk5ltA3LA3Chaw39HazdnnUjub.1
Meeting ID: 959 1352 0257
Passcode: 092744

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

Green cosmetics are personal care and beauty products that are made from natural, plant-based, organic, and sustainable ingredients and production methods that are environmentally sensitive, ethical, and free from animal testing. The study aims to conduct an in-depth exploration of sustainable consumption in India through two primary dimensions of consumer consumption: inter-generational differences in green cosmetic buying behavior and male consumer adoption of AI chatbots for green grooming products. In the first phase, the Value-Belief-Norm (VBN) theory was used to analyze the influence of values, pro-environmental beliefs (PEB), personal ecological norms (PEN), and green word-of-mouth intention (GWMI) on green purchasing behavior (GPB) among generational cohorts. The results of the survey of 401 respondents indicate that personal values have a significant effect on pro-environmental beliefs, both of which have a strong impact on word-of-mouth intentions and sustainable purchasing behaviour. Additionally, the findings revealed that these relationships were moderated by generational identity, implying that there are different behavioural reactions to green marketing messages among the two generations (Gen Y and Gen Z). The second phase focuses on the new, and largely under-researched, consumer segment of male buyers who use AI chatbots to buy sustainable, green, grooming products. The study used a dual-stage approach and a two-stage mixed methods design based on the Technology Acceptance Model (TAM) and a sentiment analysis of 3,503 real-world consumer reviews was performed, followed by a quantitative analysis of survey data from 390 male consumers by using the Partial Least Squares Structural Equation Modeling (PLS-SEM) and Artificial Neural Network (ANN). The ANN model improved the predictiveness of the model by capturing non-linear relationships between the technological and behavioural predictors. The results show that the perceived usefulness, usability, empathic ability, and responsive communication are the most important factors in chatbot adoption, indicating a preference for human-like and personalized digital interactions among consumers. This study provides a rich theoretical contribution to the field of sustainable consumer behavior and digital technology adoption in emerging markets and merges the theory of VBN with the concept of the TAM as applied to the AI mediated green grooming sector. In practice, the outcomes guide marketers in crafting campaigns specifically aimed at each generation and implementing AI chatbots that build trust and deliver meaningful results.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
About the Speaker

Dr. Shatabdi Sonkar completed her Ph.D. in Marketing from the Indian Institute of Information Technology (IIIT), Allahabad, in 2026, having served as a Teaching Assistant in the Department of Management Studies. Her research specialization lies at the intersection of green marketing, consumer behavior, and digital technology adoption in sustainable personal care products. Recipient of the Best Innovator Researcher Award 2026, she has published in top-tier journals, including the Journal of Retailing and Consumer Services (ABDC-A) and the Journal of Global Marketing (ABDC-B). Skilled in advanced quantitative methods like PLS-SEM and neural networks, Dr. Sonkar serves as an active reviewer for international journals and conferences (American marketing association) and acts as an academic mentor at IIITA.

Thursday, October 1st, 2026 at 4:00 PM (IST)

Speaker: Dr. Neha Gupta

Details of Seminar: Does Financial Literacy Influence Investment Decisions? Predicting Through the Five-Factor Personality Model
Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/95913520257?pwd=NHiOYk5ltA3LA3Chaw39HazdnnUjub.1
Meeting ID: 959 1352 0257
Passcode: 092744

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

This talk examines the relationship between financial literacy, personality traits, and investment decision-making. Drawing on the Five-Factor Personality Model, the study investigates how conscientiousness, agreeableness, openness to experience, extraversion, and neuroticism relate to financial literacy and investment choices. Data were collected from 276 respondents in Delhi NCR through a five-point Likert scale and analysed using SmartPLS. The findings indicate that conscientiousness, agreeableness, openness to experience, and extraversion are favourably associated with financial knowledge and investment choices, whereas neuroticism shows an unfavourable influence. The study also examines the mediating role of financial literacy in the relationship between personality traits and investment decisions. The findings contribute to behavioural finance by highlighting the importance of considering both financial knowledge and psychological characteristics in understanding investor behaviour and designing targeted financial education and advisory interventions.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
About the Speaker

She holds a Ph.D. in Finance from Jamia Hamdard and is pursuing an Executive Programme in Applied Finance from IIM Lucknow. Her areas of expertise include Finance, Financial Markets, Financial Literacy, Behavioural Finance, FinTech, Business Analytics, and Data Visualization. Her research interests focus on investment behaviour, financial literacy, FinTech, and sustainable finance. She has supervised student dissertations and published research in Scopus- and ABDC-indexed journals. She has also presented her research at national and international conferences, including academic forums at the University of Oxford, Howard University, and ICFAI Hyderabad.

Thursday, October 1st, 2026 at 3:00 PM (IST)

Speaker: Dr. Shivani Jain

Details of Seminar: Does Internet Penetration Improve Accessibility to Banking Services? Evidence from Developed and Developing Countries
Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/95913520257?pwd=NHiOYk5ltA3LA3Chaw39HazdnnUjub.1
Meeting ID: 959 1352 0257
Passcode: 092744

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

The rapid diffusion of the internet has fundamentally reshaped the way individuals interact with financial institutions, creating new possibilities for expanding access to formal banking services. Yet, whether greater digital connectivity translates into wider financial access remains an important empirical question, particularly across economies with substantial differences in institutional and socioeconomic conditions. This study examines the role of internet penetration in shaping access to banking services using a panel of 74 countries drawn from four waves of the Global Findex Database (2011, 2014, 2017, and 2021). Employing panel data regression techniques, the study investigates the relationship between internet penetration and banking accessibility while accounting for country-specific heterogeneity. The analysis goes beyond the broader literature that typically treats internet connectivity as one component of information and communication technology by isolating its distinct relationship with access to formal banking services. The findings reveal a positive and statistically significant association between internet penetration and banking accessibility in the full sample. However, this relationship varies considerably across economies: it remains statistically significant in developed countries, whereas it is positive but statistically insignificant in developing countries. This heterogeneity highlights that digital connectivity alone may not necessarily translate into greater financial access and that the broader institutional, infrastructural, and socioeconomic environment may shape the extent to which the benefits of internet expansion reach financially underserved populations. By identifying this cross-country heterogeneity, the study contributes to the literature on digital financial inclusion and provides evidence on the conditions under which digital connectivity is associated with broader access to formal financial services.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
About the Speaker

Dr. Shivani Jain is an Assistant Professor at Prestige University, Indore, and holds a PhD in Economics from IIM Kashipur. Her research focuses on digital finance, financial inclusion, fintech, and household financial behaviour, with a particular interest in understanding how digital technologies are changing financial decision-making and access to financial services. She uses panel-data econometrics, time-series analysis, and micro-econometric methods in her research. She has published two research papers, including articles in the Journal of Economic Surveys (ABDC A) and Digital Policy, Regulation and Governance (ABDC B). Two further papers are currently at the revise-and-resubmit stage at Economic Analysis and Policy (ABDC A) and Review of Development Economics (ABDC A). Her broader research agenda examines how technological and financial innovations can shape more inclusive economic outcomes.

Friday, September 25th, 2026 at 3:00 PM (IST)

Speaker: Mr. Arvind Mathur, Retired Director, Indian Railway Institute of Mechanical & Electrical Engineering (IRIMEE), Jamalpur

Details of Seminar: A Theory of Management and an Ancient Philosophy
Venue: C5, DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

The prevalent Management Approach in most organizations relies on inspection for quality and seeks to 'motivate' people through rewards, punishments and competition. Consequences are lack of cooperation, poor quality of service / product, lowered morale, unethical behaviour, reduced productivity / profitability and adverse effect on quality of life of people impacted.

Dr. W. Edwards Deming (1900 to 1993), first enunciated his Management Theory in lectures to top industrialists of Japan in July 1950, followed by regular visits in the following years. The Japanese followed him wholeheartedly and gave him credit for their worldwide success after the 2nd World War.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
For three decades he was revered in Japan, but almost unknown in U.S.A. It was only in early 1980s, that organizations in the West (such as Ford, Toyota, Xerox, Ricoh, Sony and Proctor & Gamble) began following his teachings, leading to transformation and revitalization of their businesses. Over the years Dr. Deming's Teachings have been validated in all types of organizations.

Towards the end of his life, Dr. Deming summed up his life's work by what he called the System of Profound Knowledge (SOPK), comprising of four interdependent areas: 'Understanding why People behave as they do'; 'Appreciation for a System'; 'Understanding Variation' and 'Theory of Learning and Improvement'. SOPK will help any organization to bring about continuous improvement in quality of goods/services, productivity & profitability in an environment of cooperation, harmony & joy in work - thus improving quality of life of all impacted.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
The Deming Transformation requires sea changes in beliefs, ways of thinking & attitudes of leaders, which is not easy. To bring about these changes, the Philosophy of the Four Yogas will help. This ancient philosophy teaches us that every human being possesses a Divine Core but remains largely disconnected from it. Practice of an appropriate combination of the four yogas (Karma, Bhakti, Jnana and Raja Yoga) helps us to restore this connection and regain our divine qualities such as joy, courage, wisdom, fearlessness, love and compassion.

Essentials of the Yoga Philosophy have deep concordances with the Deming Philosophy. The two support and reinforce each other in meaningful ways. The Deming Philosophy will help create an environment conducive to manifestation of divine qualities - helping us to live a life more in consonance with the core values of our ancient civilization. This, in itself, is another compelling reason for organizations in India to embrace the Deming Theory of Management.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
SOPK will also help heads / leaders of 'informal' organizations (e.g. families, religious groups, communities) to enable purposeful, harmonious, happy living and create rich rewarding relationships amongst members of the 'organization'.

About the Speaker

Mr. Arvind Mathur began his engineering education at IIT Kanpur and joined the Special Class Railway Apprentices (SCRA) programme at Jamalpur in January 1963. He completed four years of SCRA and two years of probationary training with Indian Railways, and completed Parts I, II and III of the Institution of Mechanical Engineers (I. Mech. E.), London, as well as Parts I and II of the Institution of Production Engineers (I. Prod. E.), London.

Mr. Mathur began his career as Assistant Mechanical Engineer, Western Railway, in 1969 and retired in 2004 as Director, Indian Railway Institute of Mechanical & Electrical Engineering (IRIMEE), Jamalpur. During his distinguished career with Indian Railways, he served in several senior managerial positions, including Chief Workshop Engineer (CWE) on the Northeast Frontier and Southern Railways and Chief Mechanical Engineer (CME) on the Northeast Frontier Railway.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
His professional journey has been profoundly influenced by the Management Philosophy of Dr. W. Edwards Deming, with a focus on its application for enhancing quality, productivity, and workplace culture. As Director of IRIMEE, he introduced Deming's philosophy into training programmes and also conducted presentation cum discussion sessions with senior railway managers and officers of the Railway Board. Following retirement, he has continued sharing these principles with government and private organizations, educational institutions and management associations across India.

Thursday, September 24th, 2026 at 3:00 PM (IST)

Speaker: Dr. Sachin Gangwar

Details of Seminar: From Optimisation to Instrument Design: Energy System Modelling for Sustainable Development and Decarbonisation
[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/95913520257?pwd=NHiOYk5ltA3LA3Chaw39HazdnnUjub.1
Meeting ID: 959 1352 0257
Passcode: 092744

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

Decarbonisation policy in India is increasingly built on cost numbers of levelised cost of energy and hydrogen, the green premium on steel, and the subsidy required per tonne abated, which are outputs of engineering models rather than observations. Change an electrolyser's capacity factor, an assumed iron ore grade, or the hourly generation mix behind an electrolyser, and the policy conclusion can reverse. Numbers of this kind deserve to be built carefully and then interrogated.

This talk presents a decade of work building such models and argues for the step that follows. Three strands are covered: scheduling optimisation and financial risk analysis for energy-intensive industry operating under uncertain electricity prices, developed with an industrial partner; multi-objective optimisation of positive energy communities across energy, economic, environmental and social indicators; and physics-based techno-economic optimisation of large-scale underground hydrogen storage. Each ends at a number that somebody else must convert into a decision.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
The second half turns to where that work leads. Electrolytic hydrogen — the shared asset behind green steel, green fertiliser and green fuels — is constrained by two things that policy actually governs: the hourly composition of the electricity behind it, and water. Using India's Green Steel Taxonomy and the first green ammonia auctions as cases, the talk argues that the open questions are no longer about the cost of the technology but about the design of the instruments meant to close the gap.

About the Speaker

Sachin Gangwar is an energy systems researcher working on the techno-economic and environmental assessment of low-carbon energy systems. He worked as Executive Project Officer at IIT Kanpur, where he led the development of a national multi-energy system model informing India's 2070 net-zero pathway. He holds a doctorate from Universitat Rovira i Virgili, Spain, in the programme in Nanoscience, Materials and Chemical Engineering, specialising in energy systems optimisation, and was previously a postdoctoral researcher at the Paul Scherrer Institute, Switzerland, on hydrogen systems within the SNSF-funded SCENE project. His work combines mixed-integer optimisation, life-cycle assessment and techno-economic analysis. Across twelve years in India, Spain and Switzerland, he has held research, teaching and consulting positions and has published in Computers & Chemical Engineering and Renewable Energy.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]

Speaker: Dr. Brajmohan Sahoo

Details of Seminar: Fuzzy Multi-Criteria Decision-Making for Sustainable Supply Chain Management: Models, Applications, and Future Directions
Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/95913520257?pwd=NHiOYk5ltA3LA3Chaw39HazdnnUjub.1

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

Decision-making under uncertainty is an important area of operations research, with significant applications in sustainable supply chains, renewable energy, environmental management, and industrial systems. In this talk, I will present an overview of my PhD research, which focuses on the development and application of fuzzy-based Multi-Criteria Decision-Making (MCDM) frameworks for addressing complex real-world decision-making problems. The research considers various fuzzy environments and MCDM methodologies to effectively handle uncertainty, conflicting criteria, and diverse expert opinions, with applications in renewable energy, supplier selection, solid waste management, and electric vehicle charging infrastructure.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Following this overview, I will discuss one of my recently published studies on optimal supplier evaluation for the organic food retail sector, highlighting the proposed methodology and key results. I will then present my proposed research direction on Sustainable Supplier Selection for Green and Circular Supply Chains, focusing on developing robust decision-making approaches that integrate sustainability and circular economy principles. Finally, I will briefly highlight my research publications and discuss potential future research directions.

About the Speaker

Mr. Brajamohan Sahoo completed his Ph.D. in the Department of Applied Sciences at Tezpur University (A Central University), Assam, India. His research focuses on fuzzy multi-criteria decision-making (MCDM) and group decision-making models for sustainable systems under uncertainty. His research interests include sustainable supply chain management, renewable energy, solid waste management, and related areas. He has 13 first-author publications, including 9 research articles in international journals indexed in SCIE and ESCI, along with 4 book chapters. His work involves the development and application of advanced fuzzy MCDM techniques, including spherical fuzzy, Pythagorean fuzzy, and interval-valued Fermatean fuzzy frameworks. He has served as a reviewer and editorial board member for national and international journals and has 1.5 years of teaching experience.

Friday, August 28th, 2026 at 5:00 PM (IST)

Speaker: Prof. Faiz Hamid (DoMS)

Details of Seminar: An Exact Approach for Solving the Vehicle Routing Problem with Drones
Venue: C5, DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

We study the vehicle routing problem with drones (VRPD), a variant of the classical vehicle routing problem, in which both drones and trucks serve customers with the objective of minimizing transportation cost. Drones can reduce operating expenses because their per-unit travel cost is lower than that of trucks. However, due to limited carrying capacity and flying range, drones must rely on trucks to serve long-distance or heavy-demand customers. Drones can be dispatched from and picked up by the trucks at the depot or any of the customer locations. VRPD is inspired by the increasing interest in commercial drone delivery by companies such as Amazon, Federal Express, DHL, and Walmart. Utilization of drones helps reduce energy usage and carbon emissions, representing a step toward fostering a sustainable and environmentally friendly supply chain. We develop a mixed-integer linear programming (MILP) model for VRPD. A number of valid inequalities are proposed to strengthen the MILP formulation. The effectiveness of the valid inequalities is verified using computational study. The computational results demonstrate a significant reduction in the CPU time required to solve VRPD instances to optimality.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Friday, August 28th, 2026 at 4:00 PM (IST)

Speaker: Prof. Shankar Prawesh (DoMS)

Details of Seminar: A Scalable Data Structure for Resource Availability in Job Shop Scheduling
Venue: C5, DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

Advancements in enterprise data and computing technologies have enabled us to collect detailed operations, shop floor, management and market data that could be leveraged to improve production processes through advanced analytics, optimization and automation. However, there is scant research on using these disparate data sources to achieve large-scale production scheduling. In this research, we propose novel scheduling frameworks for real-time, large-scale, job shop scheduling problem (JSSP) that results from integrating data from diverse enterprise information systems (IS) and real-life production planning requirements. In particular, we bring together relevant research from IS, scheduling, algorithms and data structures to develop two scheduling approaches, namely timestamp, and block and slot (B&S). Both of these approaches can schedule up to 6.2 million operations within a reasonable time honoring resource, working shifts and precedence constraints. The timestamp algorithm is easy to understand, and performs adequately in, finding a valid working slot on resources with limited quantities and updating the resource availability. However, in the resource-constrained cases, its performance deteriorates due to high unavailability of resources leading to an increased search time. The B&S is highly effective in finding a valid working slot for a resource but exhibits a longer overall runtime due to the increased computational complexity of resource updates over a long planning horizon. Notably, B&S benefits from shorter block length and planning horizon. The systematic approach for large-scale production scheduling presented here also highlights the importance of developing integrated decision analytics capability that is a prerequisite for achieving Industry 4.0 objectives.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Friday, August 21st, 2026 at 5:00 PM (IST)

Speaker: Prof. Jitender Kumar (DoMS)

Details of Seminar: Psychological mechanisms behind access-based luxury brand consumption
Venue: C5, DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

The emergence and popularity of access-based consumption is redefining the way luxury is consumed where consumers can borrow or rent luxury brands. The global market size for luxury rental was estimated at USD 1.2 billion in 2023, and it is expected to attain USD 4.5 billion by 2032, rising at a vigorous CAGR of 15.7% over the predicted timeline. This structural shift in consumption landscape from 'having' (owning/ buying to own) to 'doing' (using/ renting to access) is in direct conflict with the traditional meaning of luxury anchored in private ownership of goods for status, prestige, and exclusivity that transcends product features. Such evolution in the consumption patterns puts access-based, non-ownership styled consumption in the popular parlance for luxury brands; it is being labelled as the 'Cinderella moment' at times and presents a paradox for academic researchers and industry to comprehend. Noticeably, renting and borrowing luxury brands imply that none of the consumers (users) accessing it will own the brand legally, thereby suggesting the importance of a sense of psychological ownership in luxury brand consumption that goes beyond legal ownership. Psychological ownership (PO) towards the brands here represents a mental state where the customers consider the target brand or a part of it as theirs. This concept is earning a great currency in marketing and retailing and relevant in understanding access-based consumption. This research work specifically attempted to explore what prompts consumers to use luxury brands through access-based means of consumption. It has been theorized that this disposition is a function of developing a sense of psychological ownership towards luxury brands (POLB) and inquiry involves the theoretical portrayal of antecedents explaining this state. Data collected from users engaged in borrowing and renting luxury was used to test the theoretical model. Results indicate that a sense of control over accessed brands, intimate knowledge of these brands, and self-investment in these brands predict POLB. POLB further impacts the accessed brands' equity appraised in the form of brands' continuous usage and willingness to pay more. Furthermore, the access type was found to moderate the influence of proposed predictors on POLB as proposed in the theoretical framework. The study contributes to the nascent literature on luxury consumption through access and extends the applications of PO theory in a sharing economy. The study has practical implications for managing access-based luxury brands where managers can dwell into the applications of reported drivers and/or psychological mechanisms while designing target interventions.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Friday, August 21st, 2026 at 4:00 PM (IST)

Speaker: Prof. Abhinava Tripathi (DoMS)

Details of Seminar: Political Risk and Stock Markets: Evidence from 2024 Indian National Elections
Venue: C5, DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

Following the seminal works of Pástor & Veronesi (2012, 2013, 2020), we examine and quantify a novel source of political risk for the largest democracy India – "failure to form the government." Leveraging the surprise exit poll and actual election results from the 2024 Indian national elections and stock market reactions to these results, we answer the following questions: (1) Why did stock markets behave in this peculiar manner? (2) Which industry sectors were the most and least sensitive to political risk? (3) How can domestic and global investors benefit from these results? Overall, this behavior of Indian stock markets can be traced back to the unique nature of the coalition form of parliamentary democracy in India, engendering a unique political risk of "failure to form the government." We also show that a simple sector-rotation-based strategy can yield cumulative 6% – 12% returns around a 20-day window near election results. Our results dispel the conventional belief that Indian stock markets favor one party or coalition over the other.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Tuesday, August 18th, 2026 at 3:30 PM (IST)

Speaker: Ms. Archana Venugopal (SVP and CISO, NCDEX)

Details of Seminar: Cyber Risk, AI and Digital Trust: Leadership Lessons for the Next Decade
Venue: C2, DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

This session will discuss the evolution of the technology landscape in organizations over the past 20 years, and what the new generation of business leaders must know to be effective in an AI-driven world. It will start with the evolution of Information Security to Cyber Security, and why they started protecting data, systems and networks. It will then shift to the impact of Artificial Intelligence on business, changing the way businesses work, take decisions, engage with customers and generate value. In tandem with these opportunities, AI tools have also ushered in new cyber threats such as deep fakes, AI-powered fraud, data leaks, and sophisticated cyberattacks. The session will discuss how cyber risk has transformed from a 'IT problem' to a 'business problem' and a 'boardroom problem', and that it is a problem that needs a solution from the CEO, business leaders and boards of directors. One main area of focus will be the Digital Trust as a strategic differentiator. In an age where customers are sharing more and more data and engaging in more and more interactions based on digital technology and AI, companies are being evaluated not just on what they provide, but also on how they utilize technology and AI in a secure, ethical and responsible manner. The session will also discuss how leading organizations are answering questions of governance, accountability, resilience and trust, as well as cyber risk, AI risk and digital trust being common items on the boardroom agenda. Using case studies like the Equifax breach, the Crowd Strike outage, and AI-powered deep fake frauds, participants will explore the decisions made by leaders, governance shortcomings, challenges to resilience, and what it means to be a better manager going forward. The session will end with a discussion of the skills and perspectives CEOs, board members, entrepreneurs, and business leaders will need to ensure that the world they live in is increasingly one where technology, risk, trust, and ethics overlap. The session will start from Information Security, through Cyber Risk to AI Risk and then Digital Trust, highlighting the importance of trust as one of the greatest leadership and business challenges of the coming decade.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
About the Speaker

Ms. Archana Venugopal is the Senior Vice President and Chief Information Security Officer (CISO) at NCDEX, where she leads the exchange's enterprise-wide cybersecurity strategy and safeguards the integrity of one of India's critical market infrastructures. With nearly two decades of experience across banking, financial services, and cybersecurity consulting, she brings deep expertise in building resilient, trust-driven digital ecosystems. At NCDEX, Archana is responsible for securing market operations against an increasingly complex and evolving threat landscape. She drives cybersecurity as a strategic function—ensuring the availability, integrity, and confidentiality of exchange systems while embedding security into the core of market stability. Her leadership spans security governance, threat intelligence, risk management, incident response, and regulatory compliance, aligned with frameworks mandated by SEBI and other authorities. She has been instrumental in strengthening the exchange's cyber resilience by advancing real-time threat monitoring, robust incident response mechanisms, and secure technology architecture. Her approach focuses not just on defence, but on enabling trust, continuity, and confidence among market participants. Before NCDEX, Archana held leadership roles at ESAF Small Finance Bank, The Gulf Bank and Deloitte India. She began her career at The South Indian Bank, building a strong foundation in information systems audit and IT governance. Archana is an engineer, an MBA graduate from the University of Kerala, and is an alumnus of IIM Kozhikode. She is currently pursuing her doctorate from IIM Raipur. She is also a Board Member of the ISACA Trivandrum Chapter and holds globally recognised certifications, including CISA and CRISC. A firm believer that cybersecurity is fundamentally about trust, Archana's leadership is defined by clarity, collaboration, and resilience. She is passionate about strengthening India's cybersecurity ecosystem through mentorship, knowledge-sharing, and fostering the next generation of security leaders.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Wednesday, August 5th, 2026 at 4:00 PM (IST)

Speaker: Dr. Ashok Kumar

Details of Seminar: Developing a Contextual Corporate Integrity Measure Using Large Language Models
Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/95360845009?pwd=wya1WXblIu2gRD9UmtT2arV3Zi1wO0.1
Meeting ID: 953 6084 5009
Passcode: 617267

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

Corporate culture is widely recognized as an important driver of ethical conduct and firm value. Within this broad construct, corporate integrity – encompassing honesty, consistency between words and actions, and adherence to moral and regulatory standards – is among the most fundamental corporate values. Yet, measuring corporate integrity remains challenging, and prior studies have largely inferred integrity culture from the mere presence of integrity-related keywords. This study challenges that assumption and develops a new Contextual Corporate Integrity Score that uses a multi-agent, reasoning-LLM classification pipeline to measure firms' integrity culture. The analysis draws on the unscripted question-and-answer sections of 183,043 quarterly earnings-call transcripts from 2008 to 2024, where executives respond spontaneously to analysts. Unlike traditional dictionary-based methods that rely on keyword counts, the approach interprets language in context to identify genuine integrity-related discourse and captures four dimensions of integrity: Accountability (responsibility and governance), Compliance (adherence to laws and rules), Moral Integrity (fairness and ethical conduct), and Honesty (transparency, trust, and truthful communication). These four components are combined to produce an overall corporate integrity measure. The score is validated in two ways. First, it shows high agreement with human judgment, indicating strong reliability; notably, over 70% of text segments containing potential integrity keywords from earlier studies do not actually discuss integrity once context is considered. Second, the score is meaningfully associated with important firm outcomes: firms with higher integrity scores tend to have more independent boards and greater female board representation, are more likely to disclose and correct accounting issues transparently, exhibit lower discretionary accruals, pay higher dividends, and enjoy greater access to trade credit – associations that keyword-based measures largely fail to detect. Overall, the findings show that contextual, AI-based measures provide a more informative view of corporate integrity culture than traditional keyword methods, offering practical value for investors, boards, regulators, and researchers seeking to monitor corporate integrity and understand its effects on governance, reporting quality, and financial decisions.

Speaker: Dr. Manoranjan

Details of Seminar: From Information to Transformation: Investigating the Foundations of Ethical and Harmonious Living through a Universal Ethical Theory (UET)
Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/95618813964?pwd=bQ2ob9RtOrwshXHhE2mepiSapwJTxo.1
Meeting ID: 956 1881 3964
Passcode: 135024

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

Despite remarkable advances in science, technology, and management, societies continue to face persistent ethical failures manifested in organisational misconduct, declining trust, ecological degradation, and growing psychological distress. These challenges suggest that the limitation lies not in the availability of information or ethical codes but in the absence of an internally grounded framework that enables ethical transformation. Existing ethical theories largely emphasise external norms, compliance, or culturally contingent moral judgments, offering limited explanations for how ethical conduct develops and is sustained in everyday life.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
This talk introduces a proposed Universal Ethical Theory (UET) grounded in two foundational concepts: Natural Acceptance, conceived as an inna te and universally accessible human faculty for experiential verification, and the Harmony Principle, which evaluates actions by their capacity to promote mutual fulfilment among the individual, society, and nature. UET proposes a shift from externally imposed ethical compliance to internally verified ethical understanding, offering a framework to explain the persistent gap between knowing what is right and acting accordingly. The talk will discuss the conceptual foundations of UET, its proposed empirical validation through mixed-methods research, and its potential implications for management education, organisational governance, leadership, and sustainable development.

Speaker: Ms. Rajshree Misara

Details of Seminar: When Gamification Turns Dark: Technostress, Exhaustion, and Discontinuance Intention on Digital Platforms
Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/95618813964?pwd=bQ2ob9RtOrwshXHhE2mepiSapwJTxo.1
Meeting ID: 956 1881 3964
Passcode: 135024

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

Gamification, the integration of game-design elements into non-game digital environments, has been widely adopted to enhance user engagement, retention, and motivation across consumer-facing platforms. While the positive impacts of gamification are well documented, its potential to generate adverse psychological outcomes among users remains comparatively underexplored. This study investigates the dark-side consequences of gamified affordances by examining how rewards, competition, and feedback affordances contribute to technostress, and how technostress subsequently leads to gamification exhaustion and discontinuance intention.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Grounded in Affordance Theory, the Transactional Model of Stress, and Tarafdar et al.'s (2007) technostress framework, the study proposes a serial me diat ion model, Gamification Affordance → Technostress → Gamification Exhaustion → Discontinuance Intention, and tests it using data from 413 active users of gamified platforms including Duolingo, Google Pay, Fitbit, and Starbucks Rewards. A hybrid PLS-SEM and Artificial Neural Network (ANN) methodology is employed, enabling both confirmatory hypothesis testing and non-linear predictive modelling. Gender is examined as a moderator through Measurement Invariance of Composites (MICOM) and multi-group analysis (MGA).

Findings reveal that while rewards affordance does not significantly induce technostress, competition and feedback affordances are positively and significantly associated with it. Technostress, in turn, strongly predicts gamification exhaustion, which is the most powerful predictor of discontinuance intention. ANN sensitivity analysis corroborates these results, with exhaustion and technostress ranking as the top two predictors of discontinuance. Serial medi ation through technostress and exhaustion is significant for competition and feedback pathways but not for rewards. Gender moderation is found to be non-significant across all pathways, suggesting that gamification-induced stress operates uniformly across male and female users.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
The study contributes to the limited discourse on the unintended consequences of gamification in consumer-facing digital environments. It offers theoretical extensions to technostress and user engagement literature, introduces gamification exhaustion as a domain-specific stress outcome, and provides actionable implications for platform designers and digital marketers seeking to build psychologically responsible gamification systems.

About the Speaker

Ms. Rajshree Misara is a Doctoral Research Scholar at the Indian Institute of Information Technology Allahabad, where she has submitted her PhD thesis in Marketing. Her research focuses on gamification, consumer behaviour, digital consumption, and technology-enabled marketing. She employs advanced quantitative methods, including structural equation modelling, to investigate how gameful design influences consumer engagement and decision-making across digital platforms. Her research interests span digital marketing, consumer psychology, online consumer behaviour, and emerging technologies in marketing. She has presented her work at national and international conferences and has contributed to academic publications in the areas of marketing and consumer behaviour. Raj shree aspires to pursue an academic career centred on impactful research, interdisciplinary collaboration, and evidence-based solutions to contemporary marketing challenges.

Friday, June 12th, 2026 at 3:00 PM (IST)

Speaker: Dr. Priyavrat Sanyal

Details of Seminar: Temporal Perspectives on Distribution Channel Relationships: Understanding the Role of Relationship-Specific Investments in Controlling Opportunism Across Relationship Life-Cycle Stages
Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/92505093813?pwd=GBUYy0FKGeJkiTFvR1KIbuC97CrwnL.1
Meeting ID: 925 0509 3813
Passcode: 475082

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

Distribution channel relationships are critical for achieving sustainable competitive advantage and facilitating long-term value creation between manufacturers and channel partners. Although relationship-specific investments (RSI) have been widely proposed as a mechanism for controlling opportunistic behavior, prior research presents conflicting findings regarding the effectiveness of RSI in reducing opportunism. This study argues that these inconsistencies arise because most existing research adopts a static view of inter-organizational relationships and overlooks their dynamic life-cycle nature. Drawing on relationship life-cycle theory, transaction cost economics, commitment-trust theory, relational norms, and dependence perspectives, this study examines how the relationship between RSI and opportunism changes across four stages of relationship development: exploration, build-up, maturity, and decline. Using matched dyadic data collected from 266 manufacturer–channel partner relationships across industries including home appliances, automobiles, office automation, and furniture in India, the study investigates both strong and weak forms of opportunism. The findings reveal that the effectiveness of RSI in controlling opportunism is contingent upon the stage of the relationship. During the build-up stage, RSI significantly reduces both strong and weak opportunism due to higher levels of commitment and trust. In the maturity stage, RSI continues to restrain strong opportunism but becomes less effective in controlling weak opportunistic behaviors. During decline, dependence plays a critical role in sustaining the relationship and limiting strong opportunism. The results further demonstrate that different governance mechanisms dominate different stages of the relationship life cycle, including contractual governance, commitment, relational norms, and dependence. The study contributes to the literature by introducing a temporal perspective to channel relationship governance and provides managers with stage-specific strategies for managing channel partner relationships and controlling opportunistic behavior.

Details of Seminar: From clicks to cravings: exploring consumer perceptions of online fast food purchasing
Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/92827721462?pwd=Hnu72Emwthj3zp3NRBgOukmiZZprza.1
Meeting ID: 928 2772 1462
Passcode: 625802

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

This study explores consumers' perceptions of purchasing fast food items through online platforms. The central idea of this research is to practically assess the various elements impacting the consumers' perceptions of online purchasing of fast food items and find out the factors with high importance and performance value. A quantitative research approach was used to collect data from 402 participants in the form of a pen-and-paper-based method using a 5-point Likert scale. The collected data were analyzed using structural equation modeling (SEM) and importance-performance analysis. Theory of planned behavior and technology acceptance model form the basis for this research. The findings indicate that constructs such as convenience, perceived quality and perceived healthiness positively influence consumers' perceptions of online purchasing of fast food items. On the other hand, competitive prices, discounts and promotions (CPDP) and online shopping experience have no significant impact on perceived value for money. A constraint of this study is that it was done in a particular geographical location, which restricts the generalizations of the findings. The study only examined consumers' perceptions of online fast food purchasing, and future research could explore consumers' actual behaviors toward personalized fast food recommendations by online sellers. The research supports and extends the existing literature by comprehensively understanding consumers' perceptions of purchasing fast food online. These findings can help online fast food sellers improve their services and develop targeted marketing strategies.

Speaker: Dr. Souvik Singha

Details of Seminar: Post-Recovery Vigilance in Hybrid AI–Human Services: Directional Asymmetry, Psychological Contagion, and Symbolic Repair
Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/95653438564?pwd=YcwkL4WFIm315RCfQpf0pM9rv9FikZ.1
Meeting ID: 956 5343 8564
Passcode: 911762

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

In an era of hybrid AI-human service ecosystems, understanding how consumers cognitively respond after service recovery is critical for long-term brand engagement. Across five experimental studies, this research introduces and empirically examines the role of post-recovery vigilance, a consumer's sustained psychological alertness following a resolved service failure. We distinguish between two qualitatively different forms: defensive vigilance (driven by suspicion and symbolic risk) and proactive vigilance (driven by instrumental monitoring and cognitive control). Results show that the direction of agent transition (AI->Human vs. Human->AI) systematically shapes the type and intensity of vigilance, mediated by psychological contagion and moderated by factors such as symbolic repair, modality distance, brand integrity, and AI explainability. A final longitudinal study links vigilance to downstream behaviors, including trust renewal, choice deferral, and help-seeking, establishing its predictive and ecological validity. This research advances service recovery theory by moving beyond episodic satisfaction to explain durable cognitive consequences of hybrid service transitions. The findings carry actionable implications for designing omnichannel recovery strategies that minimize relational damage while preserving consumer agency and trust.

Details of Seminar: The Online Behavioural Advertising Tripod: Understanding the Linkages between Users' Knowledge, Privacy Concerns and Customers' Attitude
Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/95653438564?pwd=YcwkL4WFIm315RCfQpf0pM9rv9FikZ.1
Meeting ID: 956 5343 8564
Passcode: 911762

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

Online Behavioural Advertising (OBA) is a marketing technique of providing personalized advertisements to targeted customers, because it involves personal data collection that consumers feel privacy-conscious. This study aims to establish inter-linkages between the knowledge about OBA, privacy concerns, customer attitude and OBA acceptance in the context of Indian consumers. Based on literature review, it was identified that the knowledge about OBA and privacy concerns are the two consumer-controlled factors. A study framework was developed to guide the study based on literature review. With the help of an online survey, a sample of 104 responses was collected, and the results were identified with equal number of males and females. The results suggest that increasing the knowledge about OBA with reduction in the privacy concerns is the need of the hour. Marketers and other stakeholders must work to increase the knowledge about OBA among consumers. Reducing the privacy concerns could increase the positive attitude towards OBA. This will further increase the chances of OBA acceptance. The observations of the current study could be used by future researchers as well as by marketers while formulating their policies involving targeting advertising.

Speaker: Dr. Nidhi

Details of Seminar: Finite capacity multi-server queue with additional servers under admission control policy and server breakdown
Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/95653438564?pwd=YcwkL4WFIm315RCfQpf0pM9rv9FikZ.1
Meeting ID: 956 5343 8564
Passcode: 911762

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

This study focuses on a finite queueing model with multiple servers, incorporating an admission control F-policy and considerations for customers' balking and server breakdown. The F-policy concept is used to control the flow of incoming customers, making the model formulation more realistic. Implementing the admission control F-policy, along with adding additional servers, can effectively alleviate congestion issues for customers by reducing the formation of queues and decreasing the frequency of customers opting out of the queue due to extended waiting time. To conduct a mathematical analysis of the model, we formulate the steady-state equations and solve them using a recursive technique. The probability distributions allow us to develop system performance measures, including expected system size, expected number of busy permanent servers, and probability of server breakdown. These measures are utilized to assess the effectiveness of the model. The non-linear cost function is also created to compute the optimal values of the decision variables. Grey wolf optimization and particle swarm optimization algorithms are applied to deal with the cost optimization problem.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
About the Speaker

Nidhi has recently submitted her Ph.D. thesis under the supervision of Dr. Sudeep Singh Sanga in the Department of Mathematics at SV National Institute of Technology, Surat, India. She holds a BSc in Mathematics from the University of Delhi, New Delhi, and an MSc in Mathematics from the National Institute of Technology, Jalandhar, India. Her research interests include queueing models, control policies, and optimization techniques. She has published research papers in renowned SCI journals, including Simulation Modelling Practice and Theory, Journal of Computational and Applied Mathematics and Physica A: Statistical Mechanics and its Applications, and has presented her work at prestigious conferences, including SocPros at IIT Roorkee, MIMAR at Université de Lorraine France, and ORSI and ICBAI at IISc Bangalore.

Abstract of the talk

In this talk, Dr. Sourav Ray will share some perspectives on how to think about novelty in research. The emergence of Artificial Intelligence (AI) tools has disrupted many areas of learning and scholarship, forcing many researchers to reconsider how they approach research sooner than they might have preferred. The talk will be less about trying to read the tea leaves correctly, and more about helping researchers reorient their methodological moorings as they navigate this disruption. It will also reflect on the doctoral student experience and offer some frameworks for navigating the research journey in an increasingly AI-infused ecosystem.

Abstract of the talk

By integrating the rigorous frameworks of professional social work with the agility of NGO leadership, this talk demonstrates how subtle, strategic interventions—or nudges—can bridge the intention-action gap among young people.

Drawing on recent global advocacy and youth-led initiatives, participants will gain practical insights into how NGOs can support young people in creating sustainable defaults that make eco-friendly living the path of least resistance. Speaker: Dr. Raveena Gupta

Details of Seminar: Beyond the Label: How Ingredient Illustrations Stir Engagement – A Neurocognitive Exploration
Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/95175865814?pwd=9Ycz02G3nQPyYzGKOJtpTe9Jp0wW4v.1
Meeting ID: 951 7586 5814
Passcode: 690563

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

Consumers rapidly assess packaging through a mix of conscious and unconscious processes, which play a crucial role in shaping their engagement and purchase decisions. Grounded in dual process theory, this exploratory sequential multi-method research investigates how ingredient-based illustrations (realistic vs caricatured), on front-of-pack (FOP) designs shape consumer engagement, defined as a multidimensional construct encompassing cognitive, emotional, and behavioural engagements. Across four complementary studies, we investigate how illustration type influences preference (behavioural engagement), visual attention (cognitive engagement), emotional intensity (emotional engagement), cognitive load (cognitive engagement) and implicit evaluations (emotional engagement). Challenging prior findings, the results reveal that caricatured ingredient illustrations evoke positive cognitive, emotional and behavioural engagements than realistic illustrations. By positioning these diverse measures within a single customer engagement framework, the present research contributes novel empirical insights to the consumer behavior literature by elucidating how ingredient-based illustrations impact consumer engagement, especially in online shopping contexts. The research also offers novel managerial implications for product and brand managers; by emphasising how caricatured ingredient-based illustrations may strengthen the connection between ingredient and its perceived authenticity, enhancing customer engagement through more intuitive and emotionally engaging visuals.

Speaker: Prof. Anjali Awasthi

Details of Seminar: A digital twin simulation framework for assessing the impact of autonomous vehicles on agri-food supply chain performance – A case study
Venue: C1, DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

In an era marked by rapid technological advancements, the integration of Autonomous Vehicles (AVs) into supply chain networks represents a transformative shift, promising to redefine the paradigms of logistics and transportation. In this talk, I will investigate the impact of AVs on supply chain performance, with a particular focus on network design, operational efficiency, and environmental sustainability. Employing the advanced simulation capabilities of anyLogistix tool (ALX) and a hybrid discrete event simulation (DES)-agent based systems (ABS) approach, the study constructs the digital twin of a conventional supply chain network, encompassing suppliers, production facilities, distribution centers, and customer endpoints. A case study for yogurt supply chain digital twin is proposed. The research methodically integrates AVs into this intricate network, aiming to unravel the multifaceted effects on transportation logistics including transit times, cost-efficiency, and sustainability. The production flows are modelled using discrete event simulation while the impact of AVs on supply chain is studied using agent-based approach. Different AV scenarios are modelled and coupled with the supply chain digital twin to investigate the gains in profits, lead times and Co2 emissions. The resulting insights offer a strategic framework for industry stakeholders, guiding the adoption of AVs to foster a more efficient, responsive, and sustainable supply chain ecosystem.

Thursday, February 19th, 2026 at 2:00 PM (IST)

Speaker: Dr. Balasubramanian Sambasivam

Details of Seminar: Optimal resource placement for electric grid resilience via network topology
Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/92718767189?pwd=nVq2b8xVlXGaDtE5zRPudb0tMMngaZ.1
Meeting ID: 927 1876 7189
Passcode: 424035

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

In this paper, we investigate the resilience of alternative electric grid configurations by adopting a stylized approach based on graph theory, probabilistic analysis, and simulation. We consider two alternative classes of electricity network topology: binary trees and rectangular lattices. For each topology, we find the probabilities that customers located at various nodes in the network will continue to have power following a disaster, depending on the locations of resources (e.g., generators, storage units) in the network. Then, these probabilities are incorporated into the problem of optimally placing resources throughout the network. This is a cost–benefit problem that weighs the benefits of placing resources closer to customers – that is, pursuing a distributed resilience strategy – against the higher total cost of deploying a greater number of smaller resource units. Our analytical and numerical results thus shed light on the general circumstances in which centralized or distributed resilience strategies are preferable. While optimal resource placements depend on various assumptions, such as the probability that power lines fail and the strength of economies of scale, we find that distributed resilience strategies are more often preferred in the binary tree topology than in the rectangular lattice topology. Rectangular lattices feature greater redundancy in terms of paths between nodes in the network, enabling the system to be fairly resilient even with centralized resources.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
About the Speaker

He received his Ph.D. from the Department of Management Studies, Indian Institute of Science, Bangalore, where his research focused on electricity demand-side management using optimization and quantitative modelling frameworks. He earned his master's degree from the Technical University of Berlin, Germany. He completed postdoctoral research in the Operations Research and Industrial Engineering Department and the Centre for Transportation Research at the University of Texas at Austin, USA, and also held a postdoctoral position at the Chinese University of Hong Kong. His research integrates optimization techniques, data analytics, and systems modelling to support strategic decision-making in sustainable energy, transportation, and infrastructure systems. He has received fellowships including DAAD, JST, and the UT Austin Staff Council Professional Development Grant.

Thursday, February 12th, 2026 at 2:30 PM (IST)

Speaker: Dr. Neeraj Singh

Details of Seminar: Organising traditional knowledge commons through platform cooperatives in moralised markets: Synthesis, cases, and a framework
Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/97784530656?pwd=5atrsRwUtNaxpKONaYKf03yuFxjNZP.1
Meeting ID: 977 8453 0656
Passcode: 594169

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

Traditional knowledge commons (TKC) are embedded in rural communities, with collective ownership of these sociocultural resources, which contribute to the overall well-being of rural communities. However, these knowledge forms are vulnerable to erosion due to the dominance of explicit forms of knowledge under the profiteering and neoliberal logic, resulting in their neglect, poor market integration leading to distress migration of artisans and knowledge bearers, and overall structural degradation. The problem is further exacerbated, pushing TKC to the margin as they struggle to integrate with contemporary digital markets dominated by platform-based enterprises due to their inherent ‘tacitness’, which restricts their transmission. The existing literature underscores the importance of TKC for rural communities and the potential of digital platforms in enhancing rural resilience. Literature also underscores the significance of patron-run alternative platforms in the mainstreaming of traditional knowledge in moralised markets. However, how these platform cooperatives organise TKC remains underexplored and ambiguous in the extant literature. Bridging this gap is crucial for instituting democratic platforms that are sensitive to their socio-cultural valence for communities, enabling them to engage with place-based production systems, generate livelihoods, and preserve their cultural identity. In this vein, we ask a fundamental question: how can platform cooperatives organise the TKC and contribute to rural revitalisation? We adopt an abductive, multi-case study design, utilising open-ended interviews and archival data sources from three platform cooperatives: Looms of Ladakh, SEWA, and Kudumbashree. We utilise nexogenous development theory and the quintuple helix model as a theoretical vehicle for synthesising a processual framework, which is then empirically validated and refined through within-case and cross-case analysis. We establish that platform cooperatives serve as institutional intermediaries, proactively scouting TKC from rural communities, which act as living repositories, codifying and activating them, and translating them into market offerings. We also present how platform cooperatives scaffold complementors across the quintuple helix strands and facilitate interaction, resulting in the mainstreaming of TKC and contributing to the overall well-being of rural communities.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Keywords: Nexogenous Strategies; Platform Cooperatives; Quintuple Helix; Social Marketing

About the Speaker

Dr Neeraj Singh currently serves as an Assistant Professor in the Marketing Area at the Indian Institute of Management Rohtak. He earned his PhD from the Indian Institute of Management Lucknow and an MPhil from the Indian Institute of Forest Management, Bhopal. His research focuses on rural marketing and social marketing, particularly rural platform ecosystems and platform cooperatives. His work, grounded in qualitative case research methods, has been published in internationally ranked ABS/ABDC journals, including Journal of Rural Studies, Electronic Markets, Journal of Retailing and Consumer Services, and Technology Analysis & Strategic Management. At IIM Rohtak, he teaches MBA and Executive Education courses on Rural Marketing, Social Marketing, and B2B Marketing.

Speaker: Mr. Harsh Manglik

Details of Seminar: A Framework for the Lifecycle Management of New Product Development and Manufacturing through Field Operations: Essential for Orderly, Efficient and On-time Outcomes that meet Target Objectives
Venue: C5, DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

This Lifecycle Framework Would Encompass Innovation & Conceptualization including “Paper Studies”; Launch Decisions based on product viability, program economics and projected marketplace opportunity capture; Technology Readiness, or Technology Development for product, manufacturing and field support; Product definition and Realization (Manufacturing); Certification; Field Operations & Support. While the holistic nature of the overall process is critical and alignment and integration with overall objectives has to be maintained, each stage of the process focuses on its activities and outcomes using appropriate specialized techniques, methods, tools and practices. Consequently, objectives and metrics are both global and local. Such Life Cycle frameworks also require appropriate management and governance frameworks AND process and technology infrastructure. Important overall drivers will be discussed, such as “time to market”, “quality” defined as conformance to specifications, and “first time right” outcomes. In addition to product performance, reliability and safety metrics, economic and financial metrics also play a critical role. Carefully designed metrics support accurate monitoring relative to the overarching goals, as well as local sub goals. For example, metrics related to elapsed time and quality/conformance also have a strong relationship with program economic and financial performance and can frequently signal looming program risks and problems with potential impact on marketplace perception and reputation. It is important to recognize that successful product development requires excellence in each of innovation, analysis and integration, with appropriate “industrialization” of the product. Successful product systems result from integration. The concepts to be presented are generic but critical for product development and Life Cycle Management. For specific domains, they are then tailored into specific appropriate architecture, for specific product and technology environments. My illustrative examples will be drawn mainly from aerospace/defense. automotive/industrial and communications (e.g. switching computer systems, pioneering Telstar communication satellite).

Details of Seminar: Evolving Toward High Impact Leadership
Venue: C5, DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

This talk will be based on a course on leadership that I developed and taught at the Tepper School, Carnegie Mellon University. The course was offered as a second year MBA elective course. I also converted and taught this course in a form suitable for a remote online offering. That offering included both asynchronous and synchronous elements plus to two “bookend” in-person sessions. The course focuses on fundamental concepts and facets of leadership, rather than offer a prescription of How-To practices. The primary premise was that while foundational concepts are common to all styles of leadership, specific styles of leadership are unique to individual leaders, against the backdrop of their personal makeup and personalities and the context in which they operate. Another foundational belief in developing the course has been that excellence in leadership is a lifelong evolutionary process and requires the evolving leader to take ownership and drive the evolutionary process, aided by reflection, calibration and guidance.

Speaker: Dr. Omkar Patange

Details of Seminar: Technology transitions in coal-dependent regions: A model-based framework to understand the implications for social welfare and public policy
Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/92219951748?pwd=dUQfTY75DQ84MzYWx5pa9DOlIKayf7.1
Meeting ID: 922 1995 1748
Passcode: 356186

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

Coal is a major contributor to anthropogenic climate change and one of the leading causes of premature mortality resulting from local air pollution. However, coal is mostly concentrated in specific regions in most countries where the economy relies on its mining, transportation, electricity generation, industrial production and exports. The Paris climate change agreement and subsequent national pledges to attain net-zero emissions, along with recent innovations in renewable energy technologies, have put pressure on these regional economies to transition away from coal in a time bound manner. Although global and national scale climate-energy-economy models have studied the question of coal transitions, they provide limited insights on how these transitions will have an impact on the coal-dependent communities. In this presentation, I will discuss a modelling framework we are developing to study the optimal pathways for a coal phase-out within a small, open, regional economy. Taking the perspective of a social planner who maximizes regional welfare, the framework employs optimal control theory to study the conditions under which coal mining and thermal power generation are phased out, depending on preferences, technology investment costs and energy prices. The preliminary results provide a systematic analysis of the dynamic processes associated with technology transitions in coal-based regional economies, including the scope for multiple equilibria. The results are relevant for regional and national governments looking to undertake equitable and just transitions away from coal. I shall conclude the discussion with limitations of this approach and future research plans based on the modelling framework.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]

His research interests are in the areas of energy and climate policy with focus on mitigation strategies and their implications for just transitions. At IIASA, he is the principal investigator for a project titled “Modelling the regional welfare impacts of coal transitions (JUSTCOAL).” He is also working on the IIASA Strategic Initiative, JustTrans4ALL, focusing on the empirical analysis of input-based indicators for decent living standards. Monday, January 19th, 2026 at 02:30 PM (IST)

Speaker: Prof. Edward R. Lawrence

Details of Seminar: Culture and Bitcoin Price Deviations
Venue: 104, DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

We investigate the influence of national culture on Bitcoin pricing and examine how cultural distance contributes to cross-country Bitcoin price deviations. Our results show that cultures characterized by individualism and uncertainty avoidance have a significant impact on Bitcoin prices. Furthermore, we find that greater cultural distance between countries is associated with wider Bitcoin price deviations: a one-standard-deviation increase in cultural distance corresponds to a 22.5% change in price deviations. By isolating the cultural component from overall price deviations, we demonstrate that culturally driven price deviations are persistent, but difficult to exploit due to limited trading volumes outside the United States.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
About the Speaker

Prof. Edward R. Lawrence is Chair of the Department of Finance and holds the Florida International Bankers Association (FIBA) Professorship at Florida International University. He earned his PhD in Business Administration and MBA from the University of Nebraska–Lincoln, Nebraska, and a BTech from IIT Kanpur. A distinguished scholar in finance, his research interests include investments, asset pricing, banking and financial institutions, and behavioral finance. Prof. Lawrence's work has been widely published in leading academic journals. He has received the "Best Professor Award" and "Best Course Award" for his finance course consistently from 2009 to 2025. In addition to his research contributions, he has played a key leadership role in advancing finance education, mentoring students and faculty, and strengthening engagement between academia and the financial services industry. Speaker: Dr. Nivedita Jha

Details of Seminar: What Drives Cyberloafing Among Indian IT Professionals? An ISM and MICMAC approach
Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/95933249416?pwd=6e9Nq6v93p7klB96VQqEdfvbJY1DZw.1
Meeting ID: 959 3324 9416
Passcode: 789014

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

Cyberloafing, a term that refers to employees using the internet for personal purposes during work hours, has become a significant concern for organisations, among the numerous phenomena observed in workplaces. In the Indian IT sector, where technology-driven work is pervasive, cyberloafing presents unique challenges for human resource management and organizational effectiveness. Although prior research has identified several antecedents of cyberloafing, these factors are often examined independently, limiting understanding of their structural relationships.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
This study employs Interpretive Structural Modeling (ISM) to identify and hierarchically organize the key determinants of cyberloafing among Indian IT professionals. Further, MICMAC analysis is used to classify these determinants based on their driving and dependence power. The analysis reveals that cyberloafing is shaped by an ordered structure of interrelated organizational and work-related determinants, rather than being driven solely by individuallevel tendencies.

The seminar will explain how ISM and MICMAC can be effectively applied within human resource management and organizational behaviour research to capture the structural dynamics of cyberloafing determinants. Practical implications for HR policies and managerial interventions aimed at regulating cyberloafing in technology-enabled organizations will also be discussed. The study contributes to a more structured and integrative understanding of cyberloafing behaviour in the Indian IT context.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
About the Speaker

Dr. Nivedita Jha holds a Doctorate in Human Resource Management and Organizational Behaviour from Birla Institute of Management Technology, Greater Noida, and an MBA in Human Resource Management and Marketing. Her research interests focus on contemporary workplace behaviours, with particular emphasis on cyberloafing, perceived overqualification, and job crafting. Her work examines employee behaviour in technology-driven organizational contexts, particularly within the Indian IT sector. She has published in Scopus-indexed journals and actively engages in academic discourse through seminars and conferences. Her broader interests include workplace behaviour and leadership.

Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/96971128073?pwd=iAhbOuhasOs5xPY4qJ0ULabIP9zGg3.1

Abstract of the talk

The global energy transition is increasingly driven by the need for sustainable, secure, and resilient energy systems. Bioenergy provides over half of renewable energy and nearly 10% of global energy supply. India, with 10% of global cropland, generates 500 MMT of crop residue annually, of which 20% is burned, worsening emissions. National green initiatives underscore the need for efficient, sustainable agri-waste biomass supply chains with strong bioenergy potential and multidimensional relevance. It also supports reduced import dependence and energy resilience. This talk identifies key research gaps in the Indian biomass supply chain context and outlines a proposed optimization-based, data-driven methodology for strategic and tactical decision-making with a case study on Uttar Pradesh.

Speaker: Sachin Kumar

Details of Seminar: A Discontinuous Galerkin Method for Black-Scholes PDEs Modelling Pricing Securities
Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/96971128073?pwd=iAhbOuhasOs5xPY4qJ0ULabIP9zGg3.1
Meeting ID: 969 7112 8073
Passcode: 611820

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

This work presents an efficient numerical method for solving the Black-Scholes partial differential equation (PDE), which governs the pricing of European options. The methodology employs the backward Euler scheme to discretize the time derivative and incorporates the non-symmetric interior penalty Galerkin (NIPG) method for handling the spatial derivatives. The study aims to determine optimal order error estimates in the L2-norm and discrete energy norm. Additionally, the proposed method is utilised to determine Greeks in option pricing. The theoretical results are verified using numerical experiments.

Speaker: Dr. Rupika Khanna

Details of Seminar: Beyond information technology and productivity paradox: analysing the channels of impact at the firm-level
Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/99378770519?pwd=CxkQbYRjcPu028Z6PB6eNmmVWHEDQu.1
Meeting ID: 993 7877 0519
Passcode: 203140

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

This paper explores the effects of innovational investments in information technology (IT) on the productivity performance of firms. We employ a micro-level dataset of 3582 firms from Indian manufacturing spanning 1998 to 2016 to identify some critical channels through which IT investments may impact productivity. We find a significant impact of inter-industry network spillovers, suggesting that the convenience drawn from IT assets transmits to firms in other sectors of the economy without the recipient firms paying for it. These spillovers are higher for firms in the top 50 percentile of the industry in terms of their IT assets. Our results also show strong complementarities between IT, R&D, and disembodied technology purchased by the firm. We find that these technological inputs offer a synergistic effect on productivity. Our results also highlighted that these synergies vary by ownership and export status. IT also seems to mitigate the diminishing gains to R&D as firms scale their R&D effort. Overall, we show that IT investments not only provide crucial productivity returns but also synergize other technological investments to produce superior overall gains.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Keywords: productivity; information technology; endogeneity; production function estimation; panel data; firm-level
