Friday, October 24th, 2025 at 03:30 PM (IST)

Speaker: Prof. Mousami Prasad (DoMS)

Details of Seminar: The role of institutions and public policies in catalyzing solar power growth in India
Venue:  C5, DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

Solar started as an additional energy source in India's energy mix but has transitioned into a strategic energy source and a key element of India's energy and climate commitments. Solar capacity has reached half of India's coal capacity and generates about 8% of electricity. While prior studies discuss opportunities, challenges, and policies, systematic evaluations remain scarce. This study adopts an institutional approach to analyze India's solar sector growth using latest numbers available in the reviewed literature, discuss future challenges, and the impacts on emissions and jobs. Using mixed-method research, including discourse analysis, we find purpose-built entities have been at the centre of solar growth in a coordinated effort from other layered organizations to target specific solar applications. Much of the growth came from lateral consensus-building and coordination on solar policies across the central government agencies. Institutions and policies supported mainstreaming solar by increasing installation, generation, and use of solar as an energy source. The focus on domestically produced solar panels and other equipment is now gaining traction. We find that from 2010 to 2024, solarization has saved 409–488 Mt CO2 emissions and created over 21,000 full-time and 210–262 thousand temporary jobs. We identify four challenges that could adversely affect the speed and scale of future solar growth, namely land availability, investment needs, structural issues, and target setting, called the ‘LIST challenges in the solar sector’. The growth in solar now requires consensus-building and coordination amongst national entities and subnational entities and policies to catalyze the strategic integration of solar.

Friday, October 17th, 2025 at 03:30 PM (IST)

Speaker: Prof. Sushruth Ravish (HSS) and Prof. Jothsna Rajan (DoMS)

Details of Seminar: Kyon Anon? Rethinking the Whys of Conspiracy Theories
Venue:  C5, DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

Conspiracy theories are narratives that claim that powerful, hidden, and evil forces act behind the scenes in society. Much of the academic literature has so far focused on definitional concerns and the seemingly puzzling question of why otherwise rational people may hold such irrational beliefs. Consequently, remedial approaches to the social problem of conspiratorial thinking have pivoted on fact-checking and debunking the false beliefs (we call these belief-driven approaches). However, as has been well-documented by now, belief-driven approaches often fail to disabuse adherents of their conspiratorial notions, and may even backfire. We argue that the failure of belief-driven approaches in addressing conspiracy theories is arising from the failure to recognize the heterogeneity and plurality in the motivations of the members in a conspiracy community. We develop the concept of a conspiracy community as a loosely held coalition of actors who signal their allegiance by narrative repetition. Considering the functional roles of the members in a conspiracy community, we may identify epistemic manipulators, foot soldiers, and truth seekers among others. Membership in the conspiracy communities may not be hinged necessarily on belief, instead these communities may be held together by opportunistic compatibility of goals. Therefore, responses to conspiratorial thinking must also be multi-pronged and heterogeneous, taking into account not just the type of the conspiracy theory, but also the position of the actor in the conspiracy community.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]

Friday, September 26th, 2025 at 05:00 PM (IST)

Speaker: Prof. Sri Vanamalla V, Department of Management Sciences, IIT Kanpur

Details of Seminar: Remanufacturing Strategy of an OEM Contracting Third-party Collector in a Reverse Supply Chain
Venue:  C5, DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

Remanufacturing helps firms achieve sustainability and gives them a competitive advantage. Many original equipment manufacturers (OEMs) acquire used-products from third-party collectors (3PCs) due to benefit from economies of scale. In this study, we consider an OEM who remanufactures two types of products– high-grade and low-grade, using high-quality and low-quality used-products, respectively. The OEM faces two types of 3PCs: high-type 3PC and low-type 3PC. The used-products supplied from high-type 3PC have a higher proportion of high-quality used-products than those from low-type 3PC. We first explore the complete information framework in which the OEM knows the 3PC's type (high or low). However, often the OEM is unaware of the type of 3PC and this information is crucial in determining the proportion of high-quality used-products being collected. Hence, in an incomplete information framework we design a menu of contracts that OEM offers to 3PC. The choice from the menu of contracts reveals the type of 3PC and assigns 3PC with the appropriate contract. We extend the complete information case to allow remanufacturing of both high and low-grade products from any quality level of used-products. We also extend the incomplete information case to the situation when the OEM contracts 3PC, whose efficiency levels lie on a continuum. We find that the profit of the OEM increases with high-quality used-products up to a certain threshold. Further, we observe that 3PC's effort does not increase the proportion of high-quality used-products. However, there is scope for better outcomes if OEM invests in marketing strategies and R&D.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]

Wednesday, September 17th, 2025 at 3:30 PM (IST)

Speaker: Dr. Aghila Sasidharan, Assistant Professor, Indian Institute of Forest Management, Bhopal

Details of Seminar: Does Audit Committee Moderate the Impact of Related Party Transactions on Firm Value? Evidence from BRICS
Zoom Meeting Link https://zoom.us/j/97106051730?pwd=xAgnfRTnj4RvDgNZuKayxp0HXi1mTS.1
Meeting ID: 971 0605 1730
Passcode: 416900

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

This study examines if governance through audit committee impacts the effect of RPTs (Related party transactions) on firm performance in India and China. This is the first study to examine the impact of audit committee characteristics such as audit committee size, audit committee independence and the presence of women director in the audit committee on RPTs in the context of India and China. The use of the DID (difference in difference) approach to examine the impact of audit committee on RPT makes this study different from other studies. This study further examines if audit committee moderates the impact of RPTs on firm performance. This study is also distinctive due to compiling of data on audit committee characteristics such as number of directors in the audit committee and number of independent directors in the audit committee and hand collected data on number of women directors in the audit committee. The study contributes to the literature that evaluates the impact of regulations in better governance to reduce scandals and protect the investors.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Our results show that among audit committee characteristics, audit committee independence and women directors have positive impact on RPTs in India which indicates that audit committee members and women directors in the audit committee are encouraging RPTs to transfer the resources among the firms. We further find that audit committee independence and women directors in the audit committee have negative impact on tunnelling in India implying that independent directors in the audit committee are mitigating the tunneling activities through their monitoring. Overall, the results are consistent with agency theory, which states that the presence of audit committee in the board can ensure the reliability of financial statements and protects interest of minority shareholders. RPTs and tunneling are found to decrease performance of the firm in India and China. The interaction effect of audit committee independence and RPTs shows that audit committee with more independent directors are encouraging RPTs in India to enhance firm performance. Similar is the result for the interaction effect of presence of women directors and RPTs in India. The interaction effect of audit committee size and tunneling have negative impact on firm performance in India and China which indicates that audit committee members are unable to constrain tunneling activities since they are protecting the interest of the controlling shareholders than minority shareholders. But the interaction effect of presence of women directors in the audit committee and tunneling has positive impact on firm performance in India and China, indicating that women directors are minimising tunnelling kind of activities to enhance firm performance. Overall, the findings suggest that the audit committee members and the presence of women directors in the audit committee can reduce the financial misreporting on RPTs, which in turns improves the performance of the firm

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
The results imply that proper monitoring by the audit committee ensures investors protection and the firms are complying with the regulatory framework in India and China. Hence, regulators may have to evolve different audit committee norms based on value of RPTs to achieve the purpose of effective monitoring. Further, this study can be extended other advanced economies, which lacks evidence on related party transactions and role of audit committee.

About the Speaker

Dr. Aghila Sasidharan is an Assistant Professor in the area of Financial Management at the Indian Institute of Forest Management (IIFM), Bhopal. She has over four years of prior academic experience at the Jindal Global Business School, O.P. Jindal Global University, and currently serves as a Visiting Faculty at Nalanda University (Rajgir, Bihar), IIT Guwahati, NIAM Jaipur, and IIT Patna.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]

She is passionate about teaching and mentorship and is known for using innovative pedagogical approaches that encourage critical thinking and practical application of financial theories.

Dr. Sasidharan did her Ph.D. from the Indian Institute of Technology (IIT) Madras, where her doctoral research focused on Corporate Governance and Corporate Finance. She has published several research papers in reputed journals and actively presents her work at national and international forums.

Speaker: Prof. Soumyendranath Sikdar, Professor of Economics (Retd.), Indian Institute of Management - Calcutta

Details of Seminar: India in the New Global Order: Some Reflections
Venue:  C5, DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

The old global order was the one dominated by the Washington Consensus of 1989, which imposed a draconian regime of de-regulation and liberalisation (both internal and external) on the emerging market economies that wanted help from the two global institutions, the World Bank and the IMF. It was hoped that unshackling of free enterprise forces will cure the evils of widespread poverty and stagnation, the two major problems that the old regimes of state led development had failed to solve.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
But the new dispensation failed to deliver adequately on its promises and resentment and pushbacks emerged within a short time. Prominent features of the new global order are rolling back de-regulation of the labour market and the rigours of 'fiscal discipline', rethinking on inequality and environmental damage and retreat into regionalism in foreign trade.

India's gains have been mixed, some very positive ones going with deeply negative aspects. As it dreams of being a 'fully developed' nation by 2047, serious challenges will have to tackled. These factors will be discussed in the context of the current world situation where the USA has opted to go back to mercantilist anti-trade policies.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]

Friday, August 1st, 2025 at 03:30 PM - 04:30 PM (IST)

Speaker: Prof. Raghu Nandan Sengupta, Professor, Department of Management Sciences - IIT Kanpur

Details of Seminar: Large Language Model Inference Under Bursty Workload Distribution
Venue:  C5, DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

Talk 1: Large Language Model Inference Under Bursty Workload Distribution

Abstract: Large Language Models (LLMs) such as ChatGPT and Claude have rapidly gained popularity for information retrieval and problem-solving. To support their growing demand, recent research has focused on optimizing LLM inference through improved scheduling algorithms, achieving notable gains in both throughput and latency. However, most of these approaches assume request arrivals follow a Poisson process with a constant rate — an assumption that fails to capture the bursty nature of real-world workloads. Studies such as BurstGPT (Wang et al. (2024)) and ELIS (Choi et al. (2025)) show that LLM request patterns are highly variable, often better modeled by bursty processes like the Markov Modulated Poisson Process (MMPP). To address this, we propose a simple modification to the state-of-the-art WAIT algorithm [Ao et al. (2025)], enabling it to adapt to dynamically changing arrival rates without prior knowledge of the workload distribution. Our approach estimates request arrival rates online using observed interarrival times. We evaluate our modified algorithm on synthetic workloads composed of multiple request types with MMPP-based arrivals and compare its performance against Sarathi-Serve (Agrawal et al. (2024b)) and vLLM (Kwon et al. (2023)), demonstrating superior adaptability and performance under bursty traffic.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Keywords: LLM Inference, Bursty Workload, Request Scheduling

Talk 2: Research, Teaching, Publication: Three Pillars for Academia

Abstract: Academic Research is the process of systematic investigation of an idea with the intention of deciphering a hypothesis so that it facilitates the progress of the body of knowledge in the respective field of work. To disseminate the idea of academic work, one needs to showcase the output through various outlets namely, experimentation, working papers, monographs, master/doctoral theses, case studies, technical papers, review papers, survey papers, reports, books, book chapters, seminars, colloquium, conference proceedings, etc. All of these can be termed as academic publications which generally contain original research results or reviews existing results and are usually peer reviewed. Publications in fields of sciences, social sciences, humanities are generally categorized with respect to audiences, reach, applicability, focus, funding, future scope, etc. On the other hand, through teaching, experienced researchers transmit expertise, instill curiosity, ignite passion amongst all students who are expected to carry on the lofty paths of knowledge creation for the future generation.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]

The Octet I (8I) Model focuses on eight essential components: Induction, Indication, Incorporation, Innovation, Inculcate, Inference, Invention, and Inform.

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

This section highlights two interlinked research directions. The first part presents my Doctoral work focused on designing metaheuristic algorithms for solving constrained optimization problems, emphasizing algorithmic efficiency, constraint handling, and realworld applicability. In the second part, the idea of my proposed research is mentioned that integrates metaheuristics with advanced Deep Learning architectures to predict supply chain risks in the context of COVID-19 restrictions. In other words, this work is expected to propose novel hybrid algorithms to mitigate shipment risks by predicting whether a shipment can be exported from one source to another despite pandemic-related disruptions. The proposed deep learning pipeline consists of three main stages: data pre-processing, feature extraction, and classification. By combining the global optimization strength of metaheuristics with the predictive accuracy of deep learning, this research aims to provide valuable insights for resilient supply chain decision-making under uncertainty.

Abstract of the talk

Maritime freight is a crucial part of global trade due to its cost-effectiveness and efficiency. However, as international trade expands, emissions from ships are rising, especially near coastal areas, leading to environmental concerns. To tackle these challenges, Industry 4.0 enabled key digital technologies - such as Blockchain, IoT, Artificial Intelligence, and Big Data - offer promising solutions. These tools help improve ship operations, reduce delays, save energy, and cut emissions. This talk explores how integrating these technologies can make maritime freight efficient and sustainable. The study applies descriptive, predictive, and prescriptive analyses to examine trends, predict emissions, and suggest sustainable strategies. It aims to guide freight managers and policymakers in developing effective, technology-driven approaches for a more sustainable maritime sector.

Speaker: Dr. Amit Kumar

Details of Seminar: An Assessment of National Food Security Act: Does It Improved the Life conditions of People?
Zoom Meeting Link:  https://zoom.us/j/98045340901?pwd=b27gdLJHpp8F5V8aPFLKo0STe3guMS.1
Meeting ID:  980 4534 0901
Passcode: 542791

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

This thesis contains four inter-related studies. The first study assesses the social impact of National Food Security Act (NFSA), 2013. Nine hundred fifty-seven households were surveyed from Bihar ad Haryana. Despite the increase in availability and accessibility of food grains, the households were not getting the recommended amount of food grains in the scheduled time and more than 80% of the beneficiary and of non-beneficiary families had calorie deficits. The beneficiaries are close to the non-beneficiaries on calorie intake and BMI. The second study examines the factors associated with food security after three years of implementation of the NFSA. Fifteen hundred and fourteen households from Bihar, Uttar Pradesh, and Haryana states of India were surveyed. Data were analysed using binary logistic regression. Findings suggest that compared to non-beneficiaries, the beneficiaries had more employment in families, normal and deficit calorie intake, used cow dung cake as fuel, more children enrolment in primary and secondary schools, and adoption of family planning measures. The third study reviews the food security status during the COVID-19. Food insecurity occurred due to disruptions in food production, food supply chain, implementation of the National Food Security Act (NFSA), employment, and income. Lockdown had adversely affected the sowing of crops and harvesting in India apart from food processing and supply chain. Distribution of free rations till November 2020, apart from entitlement under NFSA did not solve the problem of food insecurity. Retrenchment of workers from firms, migration to their villages, and saturation of rural farm wages have decreased people’s income and changed their food security status. The fourth study examines the impact of food insecurity on the mental health and cognitive abilities of subgroups of younger (10-14 years) and older (15-19 years) adolescents Younger and older adolescents participated, 100 each from food insecure and food secure families. On mental health, food insecure adolescents are found to be more anxious and depressed than food secure adolescents and younger adolescents were more anxious and depressed than older adolescents. On cognitive abilities, food secure adolescents faster complete the design configuration, and auditory sequence than food insecure adolescents. Older adolescents performed better than younger adolescents on design configuration. It improves the capabilities on arresting hunger, furthering employment and income, improving physical, mental health, and cognitive competencies, increasing school attendance of children, and making people aware of social and physical environment. Implications of findings are discussed.

Thursday, April 24th, 2025 at 03:00 PM (IST)

Speaker: Dr. Alisha Dhal

Details of Seminar: Even Steven Wins: Why consumers prefer equal allocation from agentic AI advisors
Venue: C5, DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

AI based service recommendation platforms often advise individuals on how to allocate their resources. For example, advisory platform might advise individuals on how to allocate financial resources across investment portfolios or how to allocate time across different activities. If individuals do not like the advice of the agentic AI, they may not follow such advice, and as such, it becomes important for AI based service platforms to understand what kind of resource allocation advice is acceptable to individuals. While resource allocation can happen in various ways, this research shows that the acceptability of the advice is highest when recommendation received from agentic AI is such that it structures the resources equally across all the options. Five experimental studies that cover a range of contexts, including time allocation and financial planning show that consumers prefer resource allocation plans that follow the equal allocation of resources rather than an unequal allocation of resources. This research also establishes that perceived structure and ease of justification mediates the relationship between the type of allocation and the acceptability of the plan. Equal allocation is seen as more structured and is also seen to be easy to justify. This preference for purchasing plans with equal allocation of resources attenuates if the avenues for the allocation are categorized. Also, when individuals have a complex decision objective the acceptability for equal-allocation plan reduces. The results contribute to the rather nascent literature on acceptability of advice from agentic advisors. Individuals typically use and also like an "equal division approach" in decision-making, including resource allocation and purchasing plans. This research contributes to consumer behavior, service marketing and decision-making processes.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
About the Speaker

Dr. Alisha Dhal is currently serving as an Assistant Professor (Marketing) at the Institute of Management Technology, Ghaziabad. She holds a Ph.D. in Marketing from IIM Indore. Her research interests include consumer decision-making, goal pursuit, loyalty programs, joint consumption, referral mechanisms, resource allocation, and AI-driven recommendations— primarily explored through experimental methods. She has taught courses such as Integrated Marketing Communication, Critical Analytical Thinking, and Marketing Management. Her work has been published in journals like the Australasian Marketing Journal and Journal of Public Affairs, and she has presented at reputed conferences including the Association for Consumer Research (ACR), Society for Consumer Psychology (SCP), NASMEI, and INDAM. She is keen to offer courses like Business Research Methods, Neuro Pricing, New Product Development, and AI and decision making.

Tuesday, April 8th, 2025 at 12:00- 12:45 PM (IST)

Speaker: Dr. Sibasish Dhibar

Details of Seminar: Multi-Objective Optimization of Warehouse Management Using GA and NSGA-II with Integrated Retrial Queueing Systems
Zoom Meeting Link:  https://zoom.us/j/94833538280?pwd=a8ZW0kTRohG4owVj3HevEbZ9H7VtTu.1
Meeting ID:  948 3353 8280
Passcode: 248061

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

Product handling systems are integral to the operational efficiency of logistics within warehouse management organizations, ensuring optimal flow, storage, and retrieval of goods. This research work proposes a novel queueing system for a warehouse management system (WMS), where order processing times are categorized as premium or ordinary based on retailer preferences. The proposed model considers a double-orbit retrial of a single server queueing system with two types of retailers, incorporating the additional services. The arrival process follows a Poisson distribution, while the service process for both types of retailers follows an exponential distribution. The system includes facilities for premium and ordinary waiting spaces for the retailers. If one of the retailers is not satisfied after the first service, they may request an additional service. The queue size distribution and various performance metrics are evaluated using the probability generating function approach. Numerical experiments are conducted using illustrative examples. We first formulate a single-objective optimization problem to minimize the total cost, followed by a bi-objective optimization problem aimed at minimizing both the expected cost of the system and the expected waiting time for retailers. The GA and NSGA-II algorithms are implemented, respectively, with NSGA-II used to obtain Pareto optimal solutions. Finally, the regression models are proposed to predict the minimum cost and the corresponding optimal service rates, given the maximum acceptable value for the expected number of product orders. The established models provide insights into the optimal solutions, and our numerical findings offer valuable guidance for decision-makers and warehouse managers.
[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]

Speaker: Dr. Minanshu Sinha

Details of Seminar: The Effectiveness of Male vs. Female Endorsers in FMCG Advertising: An Eye Tracking Study
Zoom Meeting Link:  https://zoom.us/j/97057575938?pwd=JOAWz8sa94qcD66eatnRqWjJrVTkp0.1
Meeting ID:  970 5757 5938
Passcode: 578111

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

Selecting the gender of a celebrity for fast-moving consumer goods (FMCG) advertising presents a strategic challenge. While prior research has extensively compared celebrity and non-celebrity endorsers, it has often overlooked the nuanced impact of male versus female celebrities. This study bridges that gap by combining traditional and neuromarketing methodologies. Using eye-tracking technology via RealEye, combined with questionnairebased surveys, an integrated Information System was developed to process gaze data, generate heatmaps, and correlate visual attention with consumer trust. These insights are crucial for both academic research and commercial strategy, offering a data-driven approach to optimizing celebrity selection in FMCG advertising. This research also underscores the role of Information Systems in enhancing data-driven decision-making by integrating consumer analytics, optimizing marketing strategies, and improving endorsement selection through structured Decision Support Systems (DSS) and Marketing Information Systems (MIS) in FMCG advertising.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Tuesday, March 18th, 2025 at 03:00- 03:45 PM (IST)

Speaker: Dr. Jeetu Rana

Details of Seminar:Identifying the Critical Success Factors of Supply Chain Digital Transformation for Resilience & Sustainability Enhancement
Zoom Meeting Link:  https://zoom.us/j/97057575938?pwd=JOAWz8sa94qcD66eatnRqWjJrVTkp0.1
Meeting ID:  970 5757 5938
Passcode: 578111

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

Supply chains have undergone a remarkable transformation over the centuries. Not too long ago, they were slow-moving, manual operations. Orders were written by hand, inventory was tracked in dusty ledgers, and goods were transported by rail, truck, or ship in a laborious, static process. It was a world where time was lost, and inefficiencies ruled (Forbes, 2023). However, the mid-20th century brought a wave of change. The Third Industrial Revolution sparked the rise of automation through IT and computers. This era of digitization enabled businesses to transition from paper-based processes to digital systems. Yet, even with those advancements, traditional methods still dominated the supply chains, and operations remained largely disconnected and inefficient (Gartner, 2023). The Fourth Industrial Revolution, emerging in the mid-2010s, took digitization to the next level by introducing the concept of Industry 4.0. This revolution emphasized the digitalization of manufacturing and industrial processes, giving rise to Smart Factories, where machines, systems, and humans interact seamlessly to optimize production. While Industry 4.0 transformed manufacturing, modern supply chains required more than just factory-level improvements. Hence, the focus gradually shifted from isolated operational efficiencies to a more holistic revolution across the entire supply chain.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Today, we are in the midst of this revolution that is far more profound. We have entered the age of Digital Transformation – an era where supply chains are no longer just about the movement of products, but about harnessing the power of data, connectivity, and intelligence (Calatayud et al., 2019). In just the past two decades, advancements in technology has completely redefined what is possible. Businesses are no longer constrained by time and distance; instead,

they can now predict demand, manage inventory in real time, and optimize every step of the supply chain journey (Li & Zhao, 2024).

This new wave of transformation is what we call Supply Chain Digital Transformation (SCDT). It represents a connected, intelligent, and integrated digital ecosystem powered by advanced technologies like artificial intelligence, additive manufacturing, blockchain, cloud computing, big data, IoT, digital twin, augmented reality, and virtual reality. These innovations enable greater flexibility, agility, and accuracy, improving forecasting, decision-making, cost- efficiency, and the overall customer experience (Ernst & Young, 2023; Rana & Daultani, 2023). SCDT is rapidly reshaping industries (Fang et al., 2023). According to a McKinsey report, it can improve customer satisfaction by 20-30% and increase economic gains by 20-50% (McKinsey & Company, 2023). The global digital transformation market is expected to grow at a CAGR of 21.1%, reaching $3.9 trillion by 2027 (Forbes, 2023). In manufacturing, the market is projected to grow at a CAGR of 19.4%, from $367.6 billion in 2024 to $876.1 billion by 2029. By 2026, over 50% of organizations will operate as collaborative digital ecosystems, sharing inputs, assets, and innovations. Machine learning will augment decision-making in more than 50% of supply chain organizations (Gartner, 2023). In Asia-Pacific, the digital revolution is growing fastest, with China, Japan, and India leading the way. China’s government is driving digital technologies in manufacturing, Japan leads in robotics and automation, and India’s “Make in India” and “Digital India” initiatives are accelerating digital transformation (Mordor Intelligence, 2024).

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
While digital transformation is gaining significant traction across industries, the journey to successfully implement and sustain digital initiatives in supply chains remains a formidable challenge (Guo et al., 2023). According to a recent BCG research, while 80% of organizations are pursuing digital transformation, only 30% have successfully turned a profit from their efforts (Boston Consulting Group, 2020). This gap is not just theoretical; it has tangible, real-world consequences. In 2018, Revlon attempted to integrate its supply chain with a cloud-based ERP system, however it failed miserably, leading to a sharp decline in retail sales and a cascade of operational problems. Unfortunately, Revlon’s experience is far from unique. Other major brands like P&G, HP, Hershey’s, Nike, and Nokia also suffered heavy financial losses due to poor digital transformation strategies. In contrast, companies like Henkel and Pfizer have excelled in their digital transformation strategies, benefiting from increased efficiency and profitability (Correani et al., 2020). So, why do some businesses struggle while others are able to thrive during this Technological Renaissance?  This question lies at the heart of this research.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
This study focuses on the ‘HOW’ of digital transformation – How can organizations effectively initiate and sustain a successful SCDT journey, ensuring alignment with strategic goals? To explore this, our first essay investigates the Critical Success Factors (CSFs) that drive digital transformation while fostering resilience and sustainability in supply chains. To capture the intricate causal relationships between CSFs and key objectives such as resilience and sustainability, this study employs a novel causal modeling approach known as Grey Causal Modeling. Based on Grey Systems Theory, this approach effectively handles decision-making under uncertainty, complex interdependencies, incomplete data, and multi-objective systems. The analysis is based on the insights of industry experts who are actively engaged in SCDT initiatives within Indian manufacturing firms. The findings emphasize that ‘Digital Transformation Roadmap,’ ‘Cost-Benefit Analysis,’ ‘Coordination & Collaboration,’ and ‘Digital Culture & Talent,’ are the prominent factors for the success of SCDT, particularly when resilience and sustainability are prioritized as objectives. This demonstrates that SCDT is not solely about

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
strategy or technology but also deeply reliant on the “soft aspects” like people, skills, and organizational culture that enable meaningful and sustainable transformation. The study proposes a Digital Transformation Impact Matrix, which can serve as a critical tool for answering the ‘how’ of digital transformation. By prioritizing high-impact factors and identifying key areas for improvement, this matrix can enhance strategic decision-making, providing a clear pathway for organizations to effectively initiate and sustain their digital transformation journey.

Keywords

Supply Chain Digital Transformation, Critical Success Factors, Resilience, Sustainability, Grey Causal Modelling.

Thursday, March 6th, 2025 at 03:30 PM (IST)

Speaker: Dr. Sarthak Agarwal

Details of Seminar:  Family Planning in Mission Mode: Evidence from India's Mission Parivar Vikas (MPV) Program
Venue: C5, DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk:

Despite the pervasive presence of various types of family planning interventions world over, impact on fertility rates and adoption of contraception remain low in developing countries. In this paper, we explore whether a multidimensional policy incorporating these various facets like actively enhancing information dissemination and providing easier access to contraception can be a potential solution to this problem. Using a quasi-experimental estimation framework, we show that one such multi-faceted family planning initiative in India, Mission Parivar Vikas, led to a decline in fertility rates as well as an increase in adoption of family planning methods. We find that men are more likely to use contraception and the stated fertility preferences of both men and women changed. We construct a theoretical model to show that a mediating channel for these results is information on family planning, in line with the alternative-based search representation theory which suggests that individuals may end up at a socially undesirable optimal choice as the information about the desirable choice was not available prior to intervention. The intervention expands the search set and makes the desirable choice available by influencing the societal coercion parameter.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]

Details of Seminar: Board Gender Diversity and its Implications for ESG Disclosures in Indian Companies Venue: Online
Zoom Meeting Link:  https://zoom.us/j/97025748192?pwd=scBalyX9pNHEHXzcN6POR4S1vDDHtJ.1
Meeting ID:  970 2574 8192
Passcode: 811159

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk:

"As Environmental, Social, and Governance (ESG) regulations are changing the market dynamics, there is a growing need for board members with expertise in sustainability and ESG. To address this talent gap and meet the expectations of all stakeholders, firms are actively seeking individuals with experience in this rapidly evolving field. To make this a reality, women should be given priority due to their high emotional and social traits, making them more considerate when fulfilling their societal and environmental responsibilities. Moreover, the United Nations Sustainable Development Goal (UN SDG) 5, which focuses on gender equality, seeks through its sub-target 5.5 to ""ensure women's full and effective participation and equal opportunities for leadership at all levels of decision-making in political, economic, and public life."" However, women directors account for less than a quarter, or 23%, of all board seats globally. The situation is bleaker for leadership positions, as women hold around 6% of CEO roles globally (Deloitte, 2023). In this talk, I present my research work aimed at analyzing the relationship between board gender diversity and ESG disclosures in an emerging economy such as India using the theoretical underpinning of critical mass. I investigate this relationship using the system generalized method of moments (SGMM) panel regression on a sample of firms from the National Stock Exchange (NSE) Nifty 100 Index from 2013 to 2022. The results indicate that female directors, being in the minority, 17%, have little bearing on ESG disclosures of Indian companies. From the standpoint of an emerging economy like India, which has statutory CSR mandates and board gender quotas for firms, this research adds a fresh perspective on the relationship between female directors and corporate responsibility by employing critical mass theory, which is augmented by the use of system GMM as a robust methodology. I will conclude my talk by outlining the vision and future direction of my research."

Speaker: Praveendra Singh

Details of Seminar: Random Deterioration Start and Preservation Investment for Inventory Control with Prepayment Venue: Online
Zoom Meeting Link:  https://zoom.us/j/97025748192?pwd=scBalyX9pNHEHXzcN6POR4S1vDDHtJ.1
Meeting ID:  970 2574 8192
Passcode: 811159

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk:

In reality, some items deteriorate over time but maintain their original quality for a while. However, it is not possible to estimate the deterioration start time for a specific item as many factors affect the degradation in the inventory system. In this talk, an inventory control policy for delayed degradable products is investigated, where the deterioration start time is a random variable and can be extended by making use of preservation investment. The supplier mandates that a fraction of the purchasing price is to be paid upfront by the retailer, and in exchange, the supplier offers a discount on the total purchasing price. To deal with a mixed integer optimization problem associated with the inventory system, metaheuristic optimization approaches, viz., differential evolution (DE) and particle swarm optimization (PSO), have been employed.

Wednesday, February 5th, 2025 at 3:00 PM (IST)

Speaker: Dr. Sourav Ray
Details of Seminar: Effectiveness of Omnichannel Integration: Theory and Evidence.
Venue: C4, DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

:
Omnichannel strategy is increasingly seen as crucial for a retailer to evolve and offer differentiating value to their customers. Channel integration is a key element of this strategy, referring to the degree to which a retailer coordinates functions across its different off- and online distribution channels. This coordination provides significant value to consumers, enabling seamless transitions in their journey across different channels. We offer a theory to suggest that this value notwithstanding, consumer expectations and coordination costs impose significant boundary conditions determining retail business outcomes. As the adoption of omnichannel grows, the emerging capabilities in the industry inevitably drive runaway consumer expectations, which the retailer is unable to fulfill without a high degree of channel integration. However, such high levels of integration require synchronizing myriad operational interfaces that come at high coordination costs. Using data of 81 publicly traded U.S. chain retailers for 2008-2019, we find while channel integration can deliver on their value proposition, runaway consumer expectations can dent these outcomes and that, surprisingly, advertising can exacerbate this effect. Our theory reveals how certain characteristics of coordination costs can derail the strategic dividends from such channel integration. Retailers with greater potential for coordination efficiencies tend to benefit more from channel integration.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]

Speaker: Dr. Shivani

Details of Seminar:A Study of Transportation Problems under Uncertain Environment
Zoom Meeting Link:  https://zoom.us/j/97012492599?pwd=HPMZGyoY72KM97dBMdtbbi6u0Cz5D3.1
Meeting ID:  970 1249 2599
Passcode: 651286

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

:
Operations research is a multidisciplinary field that employs mathematical and analytical methods to optimize decision-making and problem-solving processes within organizations. Optimization problems lie at the core of operations research, representing a fundamental aspect of decision-making and resource allocation. These problems involve finding the best possible solution from a set of feasible alternatives to optimize a specific objective function, subject to various constraints. Common optimization problems include linear programming, integer programming, and nonlinear programming. Linear programming finds its most significant and successful application in optimizing the physical distribution of goods, commonly referred to as the transportation problem. The efficient movement of goods plays a crucial role in the financial development of a country and can be a determining factor in the survival and growth of enterprises. However, precise mathematical models often fall short as real-life data is not always deterministic. To address this inherent imprecision and uncertainty, several theories such as probability theory, fuzzy set theory, rough set theory, etc. have been introduced by the researchers. These theories have broadened the scope of linear programming and transportation models, making them more applicable to the real-life situations by enabling the consideration of tolerances for decision variables in a natural and direct manner. Rough set theory introduced by Pawlak has higher practical implications than other theories since it takes into consideration the opinion of all involved experts and respects their knowledge by lower and upper approximation intervals, respectively.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
This work delves into the investigation of optimization problems, specifically focusing on the transportation problems, where the parameters are uncertain in the context of globalized markets and uncontrollable factors. Various scenarios are explored, including the unbalanced multi-objective fixed-charge transportation problem, the multi-objective fractional transportation problem and the multi-objective transportation problem for waste management. Emphasizing environmental sustainability, the analysis delves into a multiobjective model for sustainable transportation, incorporating ANPR technology, driving behaviour analysis, and electric vehicles. To address the complexity of both small-scale and large-scale non-linear fixed-charge transportation problems, novel variants of particle swarm optimization algorithm are developed and compared with existing heuristic and metaheuristic approaches, providing insights into effective solutions for these problems.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
About the Speaker

:
It is my great pleasure to introduce Ms. Shivani, a doctoral candidate at the National Institute of Technology, Jalandhar. She has completed her PhD research and submitted her thesis in June 2024, and is now awaiting her final viva. Her research area is “Operations Research,” with an emphasis on multi-objective programming problems in transportation and related fields. During her PhD, Ms. Shivani has published eleven research papers in leading international journals, including Applied Soft Computing, Swarm and Evolutionary Computation, Complex & Intelligent Systems, and Soft Computing. In addition, she has presented several research papers in renowned international conferences. Ms. Shivani has cleared UGC-CSIR NET and GATE, and is also a recipient of the MHRD Senior Research Fellowship (SRF), a prestigious recognition for her contributions to research.

Speaker: Dr. Saumya Dixit

Details of Seminar: The psychological influence of perceived destination attributes in 360° virtual tours on tourists’ responses
Zoom Meeting Link:  https://zoom.us/j/96569991223?pwd=GAljjM0VuwZEy3NL7S6ZbBP6OubQDh.1
Meeting ID:  965 6999 1223
Passcode: 453206

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

:
A 360° virtual tour experience integrates videos and smartphones to evoke tourists’ cognition, emotion, and heightened intentions. The present study investigates the impact of perceived destination attributes on information-seeking and destination visit intention when tourists are presented with a 360° virtual tour experience. The Stimulus-Organism-Response (S-O-R) framework is extended to investigate this mechanism by incorporating perceived destination attributes as a stimulus, tourists’ perceived confidence, confirmation, and satisfaction as an organism, and information seeking and visit intention as a response. Data was collected from 260 tourists in India. The results reveal the strong influence of 360° media in generating perceived destination attributes, which has a significant and positive association with tourists’ perceived confidence and destination satisfaction. Findings based on Structural Equation Modelling (SEM) reveal that destination satisfaction is a strong predictor of information-seeking, and confirmation is a predictor of destination visit intention. The implications provide valuable insights to Destination Marketing Organisations (DMOs), VR content creators, and policymakers for effectively promoting VR videos for tourism.

Speaker: Dr. Sujit Sekhar

Details of Seminar: When the dark one negotiates: Sacrificing relations at the altar of money
Zoom Meeting Link:  https://zoom.us/j/93256771265?pwd=FKcMPaFWFMvtmqQI72a2QWePubfEy5.1
Meeting ID:  932 5677 1265
Passcode: 561181

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

:
While evidence suggests the significant role played by personality traits in affecting the usage of inappropriate and unethical negotiation strategies, studies have rarely examined the dark core. Considering, negotiation frequently involves choosing between economic outcomes and relational gains, this research primarily examines the effect of dark core and the mediating role of relational interdependent self-construal (RISC) in affecting the usage of inappropriate negotiation strategies (SINS), at the person level (Study – 1) (n = 283, managers, India). Further, it evaluates the individual and interactive effect of dark core with two situation level variables, namely, Interdependence and Incentives in a dictator game design (Study – 2) (n = 221, business school postgraduates, India). It was observed that, while at the person level, the effects of dark core were significantly mediated by RISC, at the situational level, the increasing degree of interdependence with the actor and the additional economic incentives offered, significantly interacted with dark core to predict the endorsement of a variety of inappropriate negotiation strategies. In summary, the study examines the role of dark core, clarifies its self – interested nature, propensity for favoring economic gains at the cost of deteriorated relationships and highlights the role of two important situational elements in negotiation.

Friday, September 6th, 2024 at 3:30 PM (IST)

Speaker: Dr. Priti

Details of Seminar: Sustainability Disclosures and Corporate Boards: A Stakeholder Approach to Decision-making
Venue: C5, DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

:
As the global environment continues to change, companies worldwide are increasingly compelled to go beyond financial performance and to incorporate environmental, social, and governance (ESG) concerns into their strategic planning. In an organisation, the board of directors and the top executives are responsible for formulating the company's ESG strategy given the complexity of climate risk, evolving ESG regulations, and rising stakeholder activism. In this talk, I present my research work aimed at analyzing the relationship between the board’s structural attributes and sustainability disclosures in an emerging economy such as India. I investigate this relationship using the system generalized method of moments (SGMM) panel regression on a sample of firms from the National Stock Exchange (NSE) Nifty 100 Index from 2013 to 2020. This econometric framework controls endogeneity among the variables, which has been lacking in the previous studies. The results indicate that board structural attributes, such as board size, gender diversity, chief executive officer (CEO) duality, and independence, have little bearing on sustainability disclosures of Indian companies. However, the board of directors, through the board’s corporate social responsibility (CSR) committee, strives for sustainability practices in Indian organizations. The bigger companies, based on their assets size, are more willing to disclose on their ESG efforts compared to the smaller ones. From the standpoint of an emerging economy like India, which has statutory CSR mandates for firms, this research adds a fresh perspective to the relationship between corporate governance and corporate responsibility by employing stakeholder theory, which is augmented by the use of system GMM as a robust methodology. My work also highlights the significance of a mandatory CSR committee as a facilitator of sustainability practices and reporting in emerging economies. I will conclude my talk by outlining the vision and future direction of my research.

Wednesday, August 14th, 2024 at 3:30 PM (IST)

Speaker: Dr. Badri Narayanan Gopalakrishnan

Details of Seminar: Modeling the nexus between decarbonization and global value chains.
Venue: C5 DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

:
Globally, most countries have hard and soft commitments to reduce emissions in the next few years. While this is an arduous task in itself, with a decade of experience after the Paris Agreement, it may be noted that hardly any country has succeeded even in reducing emissions since then. Nevertheless, many countries are coming up with detailed plans to net zero pathways. The implications of these on the global and national economies as well as the trade-offs and opportunities offered by such pathways to the poorer countries in the world have not been explored yet in the literature. A related and interesting issue to explore is the impact of such plans on Global Value Chains. We fill this gap in the literature by looking at South East Asia and Africa, in a global multi-country multi-sectoral computable General equilibrium framework.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]

Details of Seminar: CNN Architectures Applied to Road Object Detection

Meeting ID:  987 1216 7436
Passcode: 668769

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

:
Autonomous driving technology stands at the forefront of innovation, promising safer and more efficient transportation systems. Central to its success is the ability to accurately detect and classify objects in real time, particularly in dynamic and unpredictable road environments. However, current CNN architectures, while effective to a degree, exhibit limitations in handling challenges inherent in road object detection, such as occlusions, varying lighting conditions, and complex traffic scenarios. My research endeavored to address some of these limitations by proposing and developing advanced CNN architectures tailored explicitly for both large and small road object detection tasks in autonomous driving scenarios. I aimed to push the boundaries of detection accuracy, robustness, and efficiency through innovative architectural modifications while considering complexity and integrating cutting-edge techniques from computer vision. Building upon the findings of my PhD work, the postdoctoral research work intends to address the identified limitations and advance the state-ofthe-art in autonomous driving object detection. We will conduct an extensive review of the literature to identify gaps and challenges in existing approaches. Drawing insights from recent advancements, we will design and implement novel frameworks optimized for road object detection. This research will focus on some key aspects like improving generalization performance through dataset fusion, exploring the depth information of the images, and addressing energy efficiency considerations. Dissemination of our findings will be a priority, with plans to publish results in top-tier conferences and journals in the fields of computer vision and autonomous driving. By sharing our research outcomes, we aim to contribute to the wider scientific community and facilitate further advancements in autonomous driving technology. In conclusion, my postdoctoral research aims to address key challenges in road object detection for autonomous driving systems by refining existing methodologies and introducing novel approaches. Through extensive validation, we seek to advance the state-of-the-art in autonomous driving technology and contribute to the development of safer and more reliable autonomous vehicles.

His areas of interest are deep learning, wireless networks, and autonomous vehicles.

Speaker: Dr. Aditi Singh

Details of Seminar:  Does CSR disclosure influence financial performance of firms? Evidence from an emerging economy
Zoom Meeting Link:  https://iitk-ac-in.zoom.us/j/98712167436?pwd=lwm4AsUa6XDGWw9bsXtTCf3hDdYEEY.1
Meeting ID:  987 1216 7436
[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Passcode: 668769

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

:
Abstract of the talk:

Purpose – This study aims to empirically examine the relationship between corporate social responsibility disclosure (CSRD) and financial performance (FP) in Indian firms.

Design/methodology/approach – Data for CSRD is collected by conducting content analysis of CSRD in annual reports of the sampled firms. A multidimensional measure of CSRD is constructed based on the stakeholder theory, consisting of six stakeholder groups – employees, customers, investors, community, environment and others. The aggregate CSRD measure is created by combining disclosure of the six CSR dimensions. Multiple regression analysis is used to examine the CSRD–FP linkage, controlling for the confounding effects of size, risk, age, industry, ownership and period.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Findings – The results of this study indicate that the aggregate CSRD measures, both for quality and quantity, have a positive association with the accounting measures of firms’ FP. However, the market measure of FP is observed to have a statistically insignificant association with aggregate quality and quantity of CSRD of Indian firms.

Practical implications – The results reveal that adopting transparent and extensive CSRD is relevant for the profitability of firms, and that government interventions are required to promote CSR programs, with a specific focus on the CSR dimensions that provide no apparent financial gains.

Social implications – This study recommends the adoption and reporting of CSR practices by Indian firms for their stakeholders.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Originality/value – This study contributes to the scarce literature on the CSRD–FP linkage in the context of emerging economies by using a more inclusive data set, creating a reliable measure of CSRD applicable to a large universe of firms and including relevant control variables that affect the CSRD–FP relationship.

Keywords: CSR disclosure, Content analysis, Financial performance, Emerging economy

Paper type: Research paper

About the Speaker:

Com Capital Markets Program at Jindal School of Banking and Finance. She holds a PhD in Finance and Accounting from the Indian Institute of Management, Lucknow (India). Her research interests are corporate social reporting, corporate social responsibility, and corporate finance. She holds an MBA from the University of Lucknow.

Details of Seminar: Climate Finance Model for Sustainable Growth in India
Zoom Meeting Link:  https://iitk-ac-in.zoom.us/j/98712167436?pwd=lwm4AsUa6XDGWw9bsXtTCf3hDdYEEY.1
Meeting ID:  987 1216 7436
Passcode: 668769

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk:

Purpose:

This research aims to develop a comprehensive model for prioritizing climate finance interventions in India, focusing on establishing essential infrastructure to support climate finance initiatives. By integrating financial economics and climate finance insights, this research addresses critical constraints in mobilizing resources for climate-compatible projects and offers actionable recommendations for policymakers, investors, and practitioners. The urgent need to confront climate change and develop infrastructure conducive to climate finance in India underscores the significance of this model. This proposal emphasizes the importance of robust institutional frameworks to effectively finance and implement climate-related projects, ensuring the country can effectively mitigate and adapt to climate challenges.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Methods:

This study employs a mixed-methods approach, integrating quantitative and qualitative methods to comprehensively explore challenges and opportunities in mobilizing resources for climate finance in India. Quantitative methods include surveys administered through online platforms and in-person, employing stratified random sampling to ensure representation across diverse demographics. Financial data analysis is conducted using various research tools, such as NVivo and AMOS, examining existing financial flows and investments in climate projects. Qualitative methods involve in-depth interviews with key stakeholders, facilitated by a semistructured interview guide, and case studies selected based on specific criteria. These rigorous research techniques enable a robust examination of the effectiveness of current financial mechanisms and inform the proposed comprehensive model for prioritizing climate finance interventions. Additionally, snowball sampling will complement the sampling strategy by identifying initial participants who meet the study’s criteria and asking them to refer other potential participants, thereby expanding the sample size.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Novelty:

This manuscript contributes novelty by proposing a novel framework integrating financial economics, sustainability, and climate finance. By prioritizing price-based national policies, the model offers a forward-thinking approach to addressing climate change while advancing sustainable development objectives. The emphasis on aligning international climate finance with local priorities and ownership represents a novel contribution to the discourse on climate finance mobilization. Implications: The findings of this study have several implications for policymakers, investors, and practitioners. Firstly, it underscores the need for policymakers to prioritize the development of robust institutional, fiscal, and regulatory frameworks to attract private investment in climateresilient investments. Secondly, the proposed model highlights the importance of aligning international climate finance mechanisms with local priorities and ensuring recipient ownership to maximize the impact of climate finance interventions. Thirdly, the emphasis on price-based national policies suggests the potential for leveraging carbon pricing as a critical instrument for driving climate mitigation efforts and sustainable development.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]

Abstract of the talk:

Stochastic modeling has played a crucial role in supporting scientific and industrial advancement by providing the tools necessary to achieve goals of interest, such as analyzing system performance and making optimal strategic decisions. This proposed work aims to contribute to the revolution of the industrial landscape by integrating Machine Learning (ML) approaches, the Internet of Things (IoTs), and big data analytics, focusing on development aspects that involve performance prediction and optimal parameter selection. This research addresses the complexities of delay service stations where analytical methodology-based techniques encounter difficulties. The study is driven by three objectives: 1) identifying the strengths and needs of training-based predictions and metaheuristics optimization techniques in stochastic delays; 2) enhancing system performance by analyzing equilibrium strategies to understand strategic behavior from a game-theoretic perspective; and 3) collaborating with industrial experts to provide recommendations for enhancing system efficiency, addressing optimization issues, and choosing necessary control policies.

Speaker: Dr. Vaibhav Bisht

Details of Seminar: Dynamic Reliability Measures Using L_z-Transform
Zoom Meeting Link:  https://iitk-ac-in.zoom.us/j/95837683829?pwd=dJodqq5LK1CsnzlOpVhB8YmwRjpXvt.1
Meeting ID:  958 3768 3829
Passcode: 287476

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

:
A multi-state weighted K-out-of-n system is of great importance in system engineering due to its generalized nature. Previous studies often assumed that all components of this system were steady in nature, but in reality, the state of components can change over time. To address this, the current study introduces an algorithm based on Lz -transform to determine the reliability of multi-state weighted K-out-of-n systems in a dynamic setting. The system's parts are assumed to follow a Markov process and are repaired only when they completely fail. This study calculates reliability, availability, expected performance at any moment, and performance deficiencies for the system. Finally, a case study of a warship's power system is also used validate the proposed approach.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]

Wednesday, April 24th, 2024 at 11:00 AM (IST)

Speaker: Dr. Poulomi Bhattacharya

Details of Seminar: Carbon Border Adjustment Mechanism (CBAM): Impact on India’s Steel Exports to the EU and Carbon Tax Incidence
Zoom Meeting Link:  https://iitk-ac-in.zoom.us/j/99007708944?pwd=bzdUVEtXK3pIR0ltREZCOVVQSU5KZz09
[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Meeting ID:  990 0770 8944
Passcode: 746217

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

:
Despite rising global steel production, the share of steel in global exports has dropped to 22%. However, the sector contributes to about 8% of total emissions, leading many major steel-producing countries to implement decarbonization policies.

As a net-steel importer, the EU has taken measures to limit its domestic carbon emissions through stringent commitments. Since international trade of goods contributes to about 27% of global CO2 emissions, the EU intends to control embedded emissions from imported goods. This is proposed through the Carbon Border Adjustment Mechanism (CBAM). However, this mechanism could have negative effects on the growth and trade prospects of developing economies like India, which is a net-steel exporter with a diversified supply chain.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Using a partial equilibrium approach, the present study estimates the export demand and supply responsiveness with changes in product prices. Using these estimates, an estimate of the effect of CBAM on India’s exports of steel products to the EU is made. Applying the GTAP-E-based model, a more detailed analysis is undertaken. The study results indicate that CBAM is likely to have a major impact on India’s steel exports to the EU, causing a fall in India’s steel exports to the EU in the range of 8-14%, which in certain circumstances could go up to 34%.  The paper also gauges if the imposition of CBAM would lead to a significant reduction in carbon emissions and finds that a reduction of 1% in aggregate emissions will occur in the global steel industry.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]

Speaker: Dr. Abhinav Anand

Details of Seminar: Federal Reserve speeches and sovereign credit risk
Zoom Meeting Link:  https://iitk-ac-in.zoom.us/j/96533775728?pwd=ZjJtR1lQS1hxL0V2OUZ3ZUJYY1l3UT09
Meeting ID:  965 3377 5728
Passcode: 627408

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk

:
We examine the spillover effect of Federal Reserve Board of Governors' speeches on sovereign credit risk and find that the tone of speeches negatively impacts sovereign credit spreads. Speeches that are forward-looking have a stronger impact. Cross-sectionally, the impact is stronger for countries with high external debt and those with high exchange rate stability. We further decompose the sovereign credit spread to examine the impact of speeches on the credit risk premium and find a significant positive impact on it. Our results indicate that while Fed speeches contain important information about economic conditions in the US, they can have a major influence on the perceived creditworthiness of other countries as well.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]

Thursday, March 28th, 2024 at 03:00 PM (IST)

Speaker: Dr. Vivek Mishra

Details of Seminar: Admiring the Villains – Silence of Observers while facing Unethical Pro-Organizational Behavior
Zoom Meeting Link:  https://iitk-ac-in.zoom.us/j/93552352619?pwd=ZDNMaDJwQ1IvME9BdTBPWlByZmwzZz09
Meeting ID:  935 5235 2619
Passcode: 563249

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk:

Prior research examining unethical behaviour within organizations, from the observing employees’ perspective, have primarily concentrated on the observers’ negative emotions and its subsequent effect on their behaviour. In this study, we argue that observers may also feel positive emotion if the observed unethical behaviour has a pro-organizational context, i.e., unethical proorganizational behaviour (UPB). Using the moral foundation theory (MFT), we proposed that individuals would differ in their feeling of positive emotion of admiration as per their loyalty and fairness inclination. Further, we propose that this admiration emotion may subsequently induce the prosocial silence (organization focused and peer focused) among the observers. We have collected two-phased data in vignette-based field surveys from 285 full time employed individuals. We found that observers experienced greater admiration in case of UPB, and admiration was positively related to prosocial silence- organization-focused but not peer focused. The loyalty moral value was found to be moderating the relationship but not the fairness. Our study underlines the potential dark side of admiration emotion and indicates how individual loyalty may protect organizational wrongdoing.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
Keywords Unethical pro-organizational behavior, Moral foundation theory, Silence, Admiration.

Thursday, March 21st, 2024 at 03:00 PM (IST)

Speaker: Prof. Subhamoy Ganguly

Details of Seminar: An analytical model to characterise consumption and wastage of fresh fruit and vegetables in households.
Venue: C5, DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Authors: Subhamoy Ganguly, David J. Robb

Abstract of the talk:

Food Loss and Waste (FLW) is a major problem for humanity, with a third of all food grown for human consumption being wasted. While FLW happens at every stage of the food supply chain, in developed economies, 40% of the wastage happens in households (FAO 2011). The extant literature identifies several factors that drive wastage in households, but scalable interventions to reduce this wastage remain elusive. We contend that the idiosyncratic nature of households’ behaviour in regards purchase, consumption, and wastage makes such interventions difficult, and that an analytical model characterising these behaviours will be helpful for furthering work in this direction. With this goal, we develop an analytical model incorporating factors (parameters) that influence consumption and wastage in households. While our model applies generally to perishable items including dairy products, we focus on Fresh Fruit and Vegetables (FFV) as it constitutes a large fraction of food wasted in households, even as many people consume far less FFV than the amount recommended for a healthy diet. Using numerical experiments for a range of parameter values obtained from the extant literature and from a survey, we identify the parameter combinations that result in larger volumes of wastage. Our findings will be useful for future research focusing on recommendations for reducing wastage and improving intake of FFV in households.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
About the Speaker:

Prof. Subhamoy Ganguly is a Senior Lecturer at the University of Auckland Business School.

Subhamoy has a B.E. from Bengal Engineering College (now IIEST, Shibpur), an MBA from Michigan State University, and PhD from University of Colorado Boulder. Before moving to New Zealand, he has been a faculty member at IIM Udaipur and a visiting faculty member at Purdue University. Prior to becoming an academic, he has worked as an information technology analyst and as a supply chain manager.

Subhamoy’s research uses optimisation methods to address problems in operations and supply chains. His current research interest includes understanding and reducing food wastage, particularly in the lower levels of the supply chains – in households and in retail stores.

Thursday, February 22nd, 2024 at 03:00 PM (IST)

Speaker: Dr. Nimita Shinde

Details of Seminar: Inexact Frank-Wolfe Algorithm for Optimizing Self-Concordant Barrier Functions
Zoom Meeting Link:  https://iitk-ac-in.zoom.us/j/92650152742?pwd=c21ldlZLSndEY1A0U3lKQlBIYm1TUT09
Meeting ID:  926 5015 2742
Passcode: 928530

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk:

In this work, we develop Frank-Wolfe-based first-order algorithms for composite convex optimization problems. More specifically, for problems whose objective involves a logarithmically-homogeneous, self-concordant function. Recently developed first-order methods for this class of problems assume that we can solve linear optimization (sub)problems over the given feasible set exactly. In our work, we relax this assumption and propose a first-order method (a variant of Frank-Wolfe method) for this class of problems. Our method relies on an approximate (rather than exact) solution to the linear optimization (sub)problem. We show that our ‘inexact’ first-order method enjoys a similar convergence guarantee to the ‘exact’ version proposed in the literature, while at the same time allowing more flexibility in solving the linear (sub)problem. In particular, our approach is applicable in both cases: when (sub)problem can be solved approximately up to (1) some prespecified ‘additive’ error, or (2) up to some prespecified ‘relative’ error. Furthermore, we also develop a first-order algorithm that can handle the situation where, with some nonzero probability, the (sub)problem might fail to generate an approximate solution within the prespecified error. The motivation for our ‘inexact’ method comes from large-scale semidefinite programming problems, where solving the (sub)problem exactly is not practical. Finally, we implement our method compare its performance against existing first-order methods, and show that the performance of our method (in terms of the number of iterations required for convergence) is similar to that of existing methods.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]

Friday, February 16th, 2024 at 05:00 PM (IST)

Speaker: Dr. Satya Murthy

Details of Seminar: Maritime Data Analytics - A Ringside View
Venue: C5, DoMS Building

Read About the Speaker
Abstract of the talk:

Global supply chains are largely driven by container shipping with container seaports being critical nodes in the supply chain network. Container ports are traditionally very capital-intensive thanks to civil infrastructure for mooring ships and heavy machinery used to handle the containers unloading from a ship or loading on it, or being stacked in container yards while awaiting the next move. Labour scarcity has also led to more automated container handling operations in ports. On the other hand, daily operational planning has also become more knowledge intensive to cope with increased variability in demand from shipping lines and shippers (cargo owners), and optimise capacity utilisation. Some of the complexities are peculiar to a major transhipment port like Singapore which could connect well over 300 ships calling regularly every week and exchanging more than 250,000 containers between them! Increasingly, Digitalisation and Environmental Sustainability are 2 critical ingredients to deliver what the customers need from the supply chains -- cost efficiency (just in time) and risk-resilience (just in case). In this context, Data Analytics capability has become very important to generate insights into the behaviour of customers, equipment, labour and planning/control policies and algorithms, and other stakeholders, and thereby uncover wastages which are also opportunities to reduce costs and increase service levels by streamlining processes and improving efficiency. The current wave of AI applications are heavily data intensive and built on the foundations of predictive and prescriptive analytics. The talk aims to introduce the above topics and highlight some of the challenges and opportunities along the way.

[Context: DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT SCIENCES (DOMS) IIT KANPUR - SEMINARS, EVENTS, WORKSHOPS, NEWS AND ANN > this week s seminars]
About the Speaker:

Dr. Satya graduated from IIT Kanpur in 1991 with a MTech in IME. Previously, he had worked with Eicher Goodearth Limited at Pithampur/Indore (Eicher Motors) and Alwar (Eicher Tractors) for a year in Automotives Quality Control after graduating from IIT Kanpur in 1988 with a BTech in Mech Engg. He received his PhD in Economics from IGIDR Mumbai in 2000 and was a PhD Fellow at United Nations University Tokyo/Institute of Advanced Studies in 1996.

He started work in Singapore in 1998 as Operations Research Consultant and since 2002 has been placed in a similar role with PSA Corporation Limited (www.singaporepsa.com), which is a fully owned subsidiary of the leading global port operator PSA International (www.internationalpsa.com), both of them headquartered in Singapore. He is currently heading the Insights and Analytics function for PSA Singapore and is focussed on container port and adjacent logistics businesses. PSA Group has port operations in India too: Mumbai, Kolkata, Chennai, and Tuticorin, and they also provide cargo logistics and trade solutions in India through PSA BDP (www.psabdp.com), which is also a Group company headquartered in Philadelphia (USA).

Speaker: Dr. Ayush Gautam

Details of Seminar:Modeling and analysis of management of solar panel e-waste: a circular economy perspective
